\begin{abstract}
Paper~VI-B formulates observational readouts of the Paper~VI-A closed-universe
source and gravity response. It distinguishes geometric outputs from physical
states, event clocks, finite occupations, detector maps and cosmological history.
Primordial spectra, expansion/ruler calibrations, K16 reheating, charge and BBN
networks, K48 homogeneous/static responses, growth and finite sky covariance
are reported on their actual validity domains. The current active grade-four
$n_4\mid n_7$ branch takes precedence over inherited $n_2\mid n_7$ or
smooth-comparison numerical replays. Full ancillary calculations and historical
counterexamples are in Paper~VI-C, not promoted to new observed predictions.
\end{abstract}
\tableofcontents
\clearpage

\section{Observable contracts, present-event selection and physical metrology}
\label{p6b:sec:organized-b1}
Every comparison must declare a physical state, clock, source and detector pushforward. Imported VI-A geometric coordinates alone do not choose a present slice or calibrate a measured observable.

The former \(H_8=761/280\) construction of a light-quark mass trace/splitting
and the derived \(1/H_8\) lithium normalization are retired.  Paper VI-B keeps

\paragraph{Release-v1.69 co-realization application.}
Cosmological windows, likelihood inputs, transfer readouts, and observation
maps are directed descendants of a supplied state/history and its generated
operators.  Their graphs and licensed evaluation-dual operations need no
duplicate occurrence, but their many-to-one readout character supplies no
Born inverse.  Outside the cyclic-lineage theorem this principle does not by
itself generate the cosmological root, primordial covariance, nonlinear
history, catalog realization, calibration, or empirical outcome.

\paragraph{Release-v1.82 cyclic-lineage background application.}
Paper~VI-A now proves that the native finite-readout terminal de-Sitter branch
generates a source-free complete parent-relative cap and that the same-action
reclosure is co-realized without a second lineage-occurrence token.  For that
generated cap, the fresh full-octet structural root has
\[
 (C_0,\mathcal I_0)=(U,U^2),
 \qquad
 \widetilde{\mathcal I}_0=\frac{U^2}{Q},
\]
and primitive Hopf/contact reclosure gives the realized cyclic configuration
\[
 \mathcal I_0^{\rm cyc}=4\pi^2\frac{U^2}{Q},
 \qquad
 R_0^{\rm cyc}=4\pi^2\frac{U}{Q}.
\]
The intrinsic homogeneous cotangent vanishes, \(H_0=\dot F_0=0\), and the
exact closed-FLRW constraint fixes the physical daughter radius \(a_0\).  The
primitive identity \(a_P=\sqrt{6/U}=\sqrt{\pi/3}\,R_{\rm mom}\) remains a reference-radius
identity and is not imposed as \(a_0=a_P\).  By rooted co-realization plus
unique-history descent, the homogeneous background seed/history on that branch
is therefore a generated descendant and is not an independent G30b
occurrence.  On the native cyclic lineage G30b primordial
state/complex-structure/covariance occurrence is now closed by the VI-A
fresh-root neutral-state theorem.  G30c sky transfer and G30d observer-axis
occurrence remain separately typed; generic noncyclic branches retain their
own root-state/history occurrence.

\begin{corollary}[Local RAR descendant status and G26 readout frontier]
\label{p9:cor:v164-local-rar-g26-status}
The local chain
\[
 B_{48}\longrightarrow J_\partial\longrightarrow K_\partial
 \longrightarrow K_{48}
\]
is a generated structural/readout descendant on the regular branch certified
by Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:v187-local-structural-rar-chain}; it therefore requires
no second physicality premise.  A particular crossing remains a G0/history occurrence.  The historical
G26-GLOBAL route additionally demanded an elementary all-order scalar source
law, same-owner return ownership and an unbounded refinement/limit.  Those
requirements are retained only for that stronger Reeb/one-sided comparison
branch.  The native fixed-environment response law is closed by Paper~VI-A
Release~v2.123; only the concrete source-to-observable evaluation for a chosen
member remains.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The first claim is the typed downstream use of the already constructed local
RAR chain.  The second follows from the later v1.92 synchronization: the cited historical
frontier theorem still governs the optional elementary Reeb route, while the
native operator law follows directly from the same-action Schur construction.
Neither branch occurrence nor a concrete numerical readout is created by local
structural parentage alone.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Readout-layer dependency criterion]
\label{p9:thm:second-dependency-criterion}
A Paper~VI-B output is native when its carrier, normalization, action/source
response maps, state/history and branch are licensed descendants of the
Papers~I-A--V-C prefix and the Paper~VI-A export packet on the stated domain,
without using comparison data to select their upstream coefficients.
A native reaction-network or numerical evolution is an execution of that
word, not an additional law.  A conditional observer/source instance remains
explicit where the particular readout consumes it.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Paper~VI-A fixes the gravitational and homogeneous cosmological realization, including the scalaron branch, conditional causal-horizon gravity kernel, and terminal response data.  Paper~VI-B composes those objects with the declared finite thermal, determinant, causal-window observational, and validation interfaces.  The literal RG/Weyl portal expansion, kinetic/coherent scalaron stress--energy
bookkeeping, late-background readout family, and validation maps are Paper--VI-B
objects; Paper~VI-A exports only the gravitational action, scalaron coordinate,
terminal constant, reheating interfaces, and background equations consumed by
them.  Paper~VI-B does not manufacture a particle-space rank from a hierarchy
label or an optical/density role from a finite representation count.  Every density operator, number operator, Bogoliubov map, finite positive transfer, Born trace, open-system map, and finite thermal functional used in that composition is inherited from Paper~IV; Paper~VI-B owns only the downstream cosmological operators, observational windows, and readouts built from them; it does not derive a new gravitational field equation or support kernel.  Therefore downstream propagation data may test the resulting kernels but cannot act as upstream selectors of their coefficients.
\end{proof}

with the corresponding asymptotic endpoint at \(j=7\).  These

\(j\), identify \(\tau_j=t\), or alter its owner-supplied event predicate.  A

in the Paper--VI-A typed ledger, its Paper--VI-B pushforward remains conditional.

unique inverse at every finite integer depth.  Paper~III-C fixes the neutral

\(U\)-owned curvature realization.  The intrinsic $\mathcal K_p=6/\Rmom^2$ is retained; the additional rewrite $U=(3/\pi)\mathcal K_p$ requires the actual cell test and does not follow from event-map composition.
Composition with a Paper--VI-B readout map cannot create a second inverse, a new

\begin{corollary}[No independent \(K=16\) physical-time width]
\label{p9:cor:k16-timewidth-retired}

 =\bigl|x_h^{-1}(x_+)-x_h^{-1}(x_-)\bigr|

when the inverse branches exist.  For the curvature band

 \qquad(\varepsilon\downarrow0).

\begin{theorem}[Ward-reduced K16 production frontier and history typing]
\label{p9:thm:v148-k16-ward-retained-history-frontier}
On the local same-owner bosonic branch of Paper~III-F v1.66, gauge and
local-diffeomorphism invariance are already native action consequences and the
third variation obeys the on-shell Ward relation
\[
 V_{IJK}R_\alpha^K
 +K_{KJ}R_{\alpha,I}^K
 +K_{KI}R_{\alpha,J}^K=0,
 \qquad
 K_{IJ}R_\alpha^J=0.
\]
Hence pure gauge/diffeomorphism null legs are constraint directions, not failed
ordinary-production channels.  Rank and nonvanishing statements are therefore
understood on the constraint-reduced transverse physical source/target
classes.  Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:v180-k16-zeroT-gauge-seed} supplies a nonzero
ordinary gauge-pair block on the stated branch, so broad \(\mathrm{ORDK16}\)
and \(\mathrm{NZ16}\) are no longer prerequisites for existence of any first
ordinary seed.  Ward identities may still force zeros or relations among other
allowed blocks; complete nonvanishing is the optional \(\mathrm{FULLRANK16}\)

Once the resulting production law and its analytic flow are fixed, the
Bogoliubov occupation, deposition, exchange, and backreaction are functionals
of the quantum state and homogeneous background.  Thus \(\mathrm{STATEDEP16}\)
is not a fourth law coefficient or process selector.  On a generic/noncyclic
branch the actual state and background remain its theorem-domain inputs.
On the native cyclic lineage both the homogeneous root/background and the
fresh-root quantum state are already generated by
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:v296-cyclic-downstream-history-propagation} and
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v2114-fresh-root-neutral-qroot}.  The K16 state is their
same-action evolved descendant on the admitted quantum/kinetic domain; no
second state or background occurrence is inserted.  Retain instead the
regular-reach certificate \(\mathrm{PROP}^{\rm cyc}_{16}\), the actual
coupled evolution and its consumed source/scattering/kinetic conditions.
A unique homogeneous background alone does not supply every interacting
source law or imply nonzero occupation on every history.  Downstream
readouts consume their joint common-history image as in
Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:common-history-readout-tuple}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Sector-aware thermalization]
\label{p9:rem:v148-k16-sector-aware-thermalization}
If an exact Noether charge survives on the occupied observable algebra, the
physical carrier may decompose into superselection blocks.  Paper~VII-C v2.48
then fixes the finite KMS target within each supplied block but does not select
central mixture weights or prove convergence from the produced state.  There
is therefore no independent scalar \(\mathrm{THERM16}\) temperature selector
after deposited energy and the block Hamiltonian are fixed; the residual is
sectorwise kinetic convergence plus any history-owned central weights, denoted
\(\mathrm{KIN16}\).  No global primitive thermalization assumption may
silently cross an exact conserved-charge decomposition.
On the native coordinate algebra $M_{11}\oplus M_4$, with a block-preserving
Hamiltonian $H=H_g\oplus H_s$, the blockwise equilibrium family is explicitly
\[
 \rho_{\beta,p}=p\,\frac{e^{-\beta H_g}}{Z_g}
       \oplus(1-p)\,\frac{e^{-\beta H_s}}{Z_s},
 \qquad Z_j=\operatorname{Tr}e^{-\beta H_j},\qquad 0\le p\le1.
\]
Neither the dimensions $11$ and $4$ nor equilibrium within the blocks fixes
$p=11/15$. Block-preserving evolution conserves that population; a particular
weight requires the inherited preparation/ensemble or an admitted exchange
process in a larger physical algebra. This is the downstream content of the
Paper~II-C observable-algebra distinction and Paper~IV's sectorwise KMS
qualification, rather than an additional reheating selector.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Equilibrium temperature is not an independent selector]
\label{p9:lem:v180-gibbs-energy-injectivity}
For a finite non-scalar Hamiltonian $H$, let
\[
 \rho_\beta=\frac{e^{-\beta H}}{\Tr e^{-\beta H}},
 \qquad
 E(\beta)=\Tr(H\rho_\beta).
\]
Then
\[
 \boxed{\frac{dE}{d\beta}
 =-\operatorname{Var}_{\rho_\beta}(H)<0}
\]
for finite $\beta$ on every nontrivial block.  Hence a deposited energy in the
Gibbs range determines at most one equilibrium $\beta$ on that block.
Central weights of exact superselection sectors remain state/history data
unless a generated exchange process mixes them, and proving irreversible
approach to the Gibbs/KMS target remains \(\mathrm{KIN16}\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{corollary}[Common-history readout tuple]
\label{p9:cor:common-history-readout-tuple}
Let $\mathfrak H_d$ be an already generated nonempty complete compatible
family of history classes for a licensed law, its occurring initial/boundary
datum $d$, and the declared compatibility/gauge conditions.  Its history
construction, continuation and quotient operations must be licensed on the
admitted domain.  Let
\[
 F_j:\mathfrak H_d\longrightarrow Y_j,\qquad j=1,\ldots,r,
\]
be licensed readouts into predeclared target sorts on a common claim domain.
Include every consumed extra
source, observer and boundary input in $d$ or retain it explicitly.  Suppose
the maps descend through the prescribed equivalence relation and collection
of their outputs is a licensed tuple operation.  Then the jointly compatible
readout family is
\[
 \boxed{\mathfrak Y_d
 =\{(F_1(h),\ldots,F_r(h)):h\in\mathfrak H_d\}
 \subseteq\prod_{j=1}^rF_j(\mathfrak H_d).}
\]
If the Cauchy/boundary-value problem is well posed and has one structured
history class, the tuple is a generated descendant with no independent
selector for each component.  If several classes remain, retain the joint
image.  A readout closes whenever its value is common to that nonempty
family; no arbitrary history member is selected.

On the native cyclic lineage, a licensed evolved-state readout uses the
fresh-root quantum state of
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v2114-fresh-root-neutral-qroot} transported by the actual
completed evolution.  No new K16, BBN or late-matter preparation is inserted.
The homogeneous background import remains owned by
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:v296-cyclic-downstream-history-propagation}.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The licensed tuple word $F=(F_1,\ldots,F_r)$ sends each compatible history
to one jointly compatible tuple, giving the image and inclusion.  Reverse
inclusion would require a common history producing every proposed marginal
tuple; separate marginal membership does not establish this lift.  This
retains Paper~II-A,
Corollary~\ref{p2:cor:joint-unresolved-family-exhaustion}, on the already
generated common domain.
If the structured history class is unique, Paper~III-A,
Theorem~\ref{p3a:thm:unique-solution-history-descent}, makes it a generated
descendant.  Paper~II-A,
Theorems~\ref{p2a:thm:rooted-generated-image-corealization} and
\ref{p2a:thm:v3101-generated-coconsumption}, retain the complete licensed
output tuple without separate occurrence choices.  With several classes,
Theorem~\ref{p2a:thm:generated-exact-solution-moduli} retains the licensed
compatible-family readout and its common generated content without a member
selector.  The joint image therefore closes only common values.
The quantum-state and homogeneous-background clauses import
the cited upstream theorems on their actual domains.
\end{proof}

This corollary does not construct a missing reaction or interaction law,
prove global well-posedness, or determine an actual observer.  A unique
homogeneous background alone does not determine every interacting quantum
or kinetic source state.  Missing source/collision maps and regular/global
continuation remain explicit.  The tuple also does not create a new physical
interaction between separate sectors.  In particular the native CP--BL2

their existing frontiers.  The BBN application uses the actual rate and

Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:bbn-network-history-descendant}.

\label{p6b:prop:v322-angular-six}
For the ten unit projective axes $u_a$ define the antipodally symmetric
unit-mass discrete measure
$\nu=(1/20)\sum_{a=1}^{10}(\delta_{u_a}+\delta_{-u_a})$.
Then $\nu$ has vanishing spherical-harmonic coefficients for
$1\le\ell\le5$.  The first nonconstant component is at $\ell=6$.
Writing $P_6$ for the degree-six Legendre polynomial and
$H_6(n)=\sum_a(u_a\cdot n)^6-10/7$ for $|n|=1$, define
\[
 \boxed{T_6(n):=\frac1{10}\sum_{a=1}^{10}P_6(u_a\cdot n)
              =\frac{231}{160}H_6(n).}
\]
Its axis values and exact squared angular norm are
\[
 \boxed{T_6(u_a)=\frac{11}{81},\qquad
 \frac1{4\pi}\int_{S^2}T_6(n)^2\,d\Omega=\frac{11}{1053}.}
\]
With standard orthonormal spherical harmonics, the purely geometric
$\ell=6$ power (i.e. $\sum_{m=-6}^{6}|\int Y_{6m}^*\,d\nu|^2$) is
$143/(324\pi)$; it is \emph{not} an observed $C_6$ prediction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The ten-axis second and fourth moments agree with the rotational
sphere averages and the antipodal measure has no odd moments.  Hence
it is a spherical 5-design.  Homogenizing $P_6$ and substituting the
sixth moment gives the identity for $T_6$; the axial value uses the
Petersen dot-product multiplicities $1,6,3$ for squared overlaps
$1,1/9,5/9$.  The addition theorem and orthogonality give
$\langle T_6^2\rangle=11/(13\cdot81)$ and the stated power.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The first oriented, odd icosahedral angular invariant]
The upstream invariant ring also contains the nonzero harmonic
$H_{15}=\det(n,\nabla H_6,\nabla H_{10})$, of angular degree fifteen.
It is odd under $n\mapsto-n$ and hence absent from the unweighted
$\{\pm u_a\}$ sky-template measure.  The signed Petersen cycle
holonomy does not by itself identify a physical parity-odd sky source;
that identification would require an action-owned oriented tensor and
its parity-sensitive observational transfer.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Low-multipole and spectral-transfer non-promotion]
\label{p6b:cor:v322-sky-firewall}
The \emph{unweighted complete axis packet by itself} cannot supply a
preferred CMB quadrupole or octopole axis, a low-$\ell$ alignment law,
a tensor-to-scalar ratio, or a numerical primordial amplitude.  The
angular $\ell=6$ template is a valid optional comparison only after an
actual $S^3$ scalar state, its linear or nonlinear sky transfer, observer
orientation, visibility and noise/covariance word are supplied.  The
existing right-$2I$ shell projector and its normalized transfer comb
are unchanged; there is no native new smooth envelope multiplier.
Likewise a $\mathbb Z_2$ Petersen loop sign is not an inserted cosmic
birefringence or gravitational-wave polarization.
\end{corollary}
% END PETERSEN-HOPF PAPER VI-B v2.322


\section{Expansion, Hubble parameters, distances and optical responses}
\label{p6b:sec:organized-b3}
The Hubble and distance families keep their stated integration constants and present-event conventions. A conditional calibration is not an unconditional prediction.

% The technical proof companion retains auxiliary original labels.
% Source Paper VI-B, original line 2097
\subsection{Background distances without an inserted optical homothety}
\label{p9:sec:background-distances-optical-transport}
On the declared conserved-dust/radiation/curvature and constant-vacuum comparison domain, the density-polynomial background and standard distance pushforward are
\begin{align}
E(z)^2&=\Omega_m(1+z)^3+\Omega_r(1+z)^4
      +\Omega_k(1+z)^2+\Omega_\Lambda,\\
D_C(z)&=\frac{c}{H_0}\int_0^z\frac{dz'}{E(z')}.
\label{p9:eq:background-distance-core}
\end{align}
For an actual nonstationary geometric H-response, use instead its solved
$E(z)=H(z)/H_0$ in this same distance integral; the density polynomial
does not follow from $H_0$ and endpoint fractions alone. The transverse distance is
\begin{equation}
D_M(z)=
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{c}{H_0\sqrt{\Omega_k}}
\sinh\!\left(\sqrt{\Omega_k}\,H_0D_C/c\right),&\Omega_k>0,\\[1ex]
D_C,&\Omega_k=0,\\[1ex]
\dfrac{c}{H_0\sqrt{-\Omega_k}}
\sin\!\left(\sqrt{-\Omega_k}\,H_0D_C/c\right),&\Omega_k<0,
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
and
\[
D_A=\frac{D_M}{1+z},\qquad D_L=(1+z)D_M,
\qquad D_V=\left(D_M^2\frac{cz}{H(z)}\right)^{1/3}.
\]
No factor \(24/25\) is applied unless an independently constituted optical
carrier map is later supplied.

\begin{proposition}[Exact closed-curvature optical response under an external clock comparison]
\label{p9:prop:external-optical-homothety-diagnostic}
Consider only the \emph{external} comparison \(H^{-1}\mapsto T H^{-1}\)
with \(T>0\), unchanged opacity and all other diffusion-integrand factors,
fixed source redshift, and a fixed physical closed-slice radius \(a_*>0\).
The line-of-sight distance scales as \(D_C\mapsto TD_C\), while
\(r_D^2\mapsto Tr_D^2\).  Put \(\chi=D_C/a_*\) and restrict to the
positive-distance first-geodesic branch \(0<\chi<\pi\) and
\(0<T\chi<\pi\).  Since \(D_M=a_*\sin\chi\), the \emph{exact}
angular-damping response is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v1201-curved-damping-ratio}
 \boxed{\frac{\theta_D(T)}{\theta_D(1)}
 =\sqrt T\,\frac{\sin\chi}{\sin(T\chi)}.}
\end{equation}
The earlier flat expression
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:damping-residual}
 \frac{\theta_D(T)}{\theta_D(1)}=T^{-1/2}
\end{equation}
remains \emph{exact} on the flat branch and on a full optical-radius
homothety \((D_C,a_*)\mapsto(TD_C,Ta_*)\), but is only the
\(\chi\to0\) limit at fixed \(a_*\).
In particular, at fixed radius,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v1201-curved-damping-sensitivity}
 \left.\frac{\partial\log\theta_D(T)}{\partial\log T}
 \right|_{T=1}=\frac12-\chi\cot\chi.
\end{equation}
For \(T>1\) on the stated branch the ratio in
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:v1201-curved-damping-ratio} is strictly larger than
\(T^{-1/2}\).  Neither this external comparison parameter nor a numerical
CMB angular shift is thereby selected on the native branch.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The fixed opacity comparison gives \(D_C(T)=TD_C(1)\) and
\(r_D(T)=\sqrt T r_D(1)\).  At fixed \(a_*\), the nonlinear transverse
distance is \(D_M(T)=a_*\sin(T\chi)\), so
\(\theta_D=r_D/D_A=(1+z)r_D/D_M\) yields the exact ratio.
If \(a_*\mapsto Ta_*\) as well, the sine argument is unchanged and
\(D_M\mapsto TD_M\); the flat branch has the same linear law.
Logarithmic differentiation gives the displayed sensitivity.  The strict
fixed-radius correction for \(T>1\) follows from the decrease of
\(\sin x/x\) on \((0,\pi)\).  The small-curvature expansion is
\[
 \frac{\theta_D(T)}{\theta_D(1)}
 =T^{-1/2}\left[1+\frac{T^2-1}{6}\chi^2+O(\chi^4)\right]
\]
for fixed \(T\), not a universal exact homothety.  This is a closed-geometry
optical diagnostic, not a generated physical deformation of \(H(z)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Cosmological Refinement Closure}
\label{p9:sec:cosmological-refinement-closure}

\subsubsection{Late dust--vacuum clock with the integration constant exposed}
\label{p9:sec:closed-curvature-clock-density-closure}
On the late frozen branch, neglect radiation in the acceleration condition and
write
\[
p_m=0,\qquad p_\Lambda=-\rho_\Lambda c^2.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:qzero-density-ratio}
q=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \rho_{m,\rm acc}=2\rho_\Lambda.
\end{equation}
The current Paper--VI-A equations leave the dust normalization free.  Define
\[
N:=N_{P\to\rm acc}=\log(a_{\rm acc}/a_P),
\qquad a_P=\sqrt{6/U}.
\]
The exact background relation is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:free-depth-rhoP}
N=\frac13\log\frac{\rho_{m,P}}{2\rho_\Lambda},
\end{equation}
so \(N\) is one ordinary FLRW integration constant, not \(2\delta^{-2}\).

\begin{proposition}[No intrinsic present-slice or dust-normalization selector]
\label{p9:prop:present-slice-no-go}
Within the autonomous late dust+vacuum FLRW equations and the fixed terminal
constant \(\Lambda_{\rm eff}\), neither the dust integration constant \(N\) nor
a distinguished later slice \(\Delta N>0\) is selected.  For every admissible
\(N\) and every later \(\Delta N\), dust conservation and the same field
equations generate a valid member/slice.  Therefore a numerical present-day
fraction or nonzero curvature requires one occurrence/boundary condition that
identifies the observational slice; it cannot be derived from the carrier
constants alone.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{p9:eq:free-depth-rhoP} exhibits a one-to-one free parameter
between \(N\) and the positive dust normalization \(\rho_{m,P}\).  After a
solution is fixed, evaluating it at any later scale factor
\(a=a_{\rm acc}e^{\Delta N}\) preserves the same equations and conservation
law.  No term in the autonomous system labels one such later event as
``present.''  Hence both selections require occurrence/boundary data not yet
generated by the autonomous late system.  Under the end-to-end closure
criterion the dust normalization is a \texttt{BRANCH} gap and the relational
present-slice identification is a \texttt{READOUT} gap until those arrows are
derived from upstream framework data.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Final-octet coordinate fixes the homogeneous curvature-response parameterization; present abundance remains history-dependent]
\label{p9:prop:k48-pullback-no-dust-normalization}
The scheme-uniform Paper--VI-A complement gives
\[
 Q_D(\varrho)
 =1-\frac{\log(\varrho/C_\sigma)}{\log\alpha_\sigma}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \boxed{\varrho=C_{6,\sigma}\alpha_\sigma^{-Q_D}},
 \qquad C_{6,\sigma}:=C_\sigma\alpha_\sigma.
\]
Together with Paper~VI-A
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v292-k48-h-modality-corealization}, the physical
homogeneous cDM readout is therefore
\[
 \boxed{
 (\rho_{\rm cDM},p_{\rm cDM})
 :=(\rho_{\rm curv},p_{\rm curv})
 \big|_{\scal=\varrho(Q_D),\,H\text{-history}}.}
\]
No independent \(\mathrm{K48HILBERT}_{\rm descent}\) is required: the
physical owner is the co-realized homogeneous $H$-modality of the existing
metric-$f(R)$ curvature response.  The finite carrier-energy current remains a
distinct finite diagnostic descendant and is not identified with the curvature
stress.  On a generic branch a background history may still be supplied.  On
the cyclic lineage the homogeneous history is generated upstream; numerical
abundance still requires the actual retained-scalar source history to lie in
the claimed final-octet response domain and requires a selected present slice.
Cold/underdamped residence, running-mass adiabaticity and well residence are
regime conditions on that same scalaron history when its coherent component is
used; they are not duplicate requirements from a second mode.  Exact instantaneous separation into a
separately conserved material-dust current is a stronger optional claim.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The complement identity is algebraically equivalent to the displayed
curvature parameterization.  Paper~VI-A already places the physical cDM role
in the homogeneous $H$ modality and supplies the exact metric-$f(R)$
curvature-fluid Hilbert tensor.  Generated-role co-realization therefore removes a duplicate Hilbert owner.
On the cyclic lineage, history dependence is dependence on the one generated
history subject to the reduced-source continuation/domain certificate
\(\mathrm{PROP}^{\rm cyc}_{48}\), while observational-slice dependence remains
independently downstream.

\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact-domain stationary source readout]
\label{p9:cor:v1103-stationary-source-readout}
On Paper~VI-A's undressed lower branch $C<R\le C_2$, put
$B=2f-RF$, $\chi=f_{RR}$ and $s=3\mathcal S_T/c^2$.  For
\[
 s\in(-C,B(C_2)],\qquad b_0=1-\frac{2C_2}{U+C}>0,
\]
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v2139-lower-stationary-source-response} specializes the
existing stationary inverse to the unique member $\bar R=B^{-1}(s)$ and gives
\[
 \boxed{\frac{d\bar R}{ds}=\frac1{F-\bar R\chi},\qquad
 \frac{d\bar F}{ds}=\frac1{3m_s^2},\qquad
 |\Delta\bar R|\le b_0^{-1}|\Delta s|.}
\]
The upper endpoint derivative is one-sided; as $s\downarrow-C$ the curvature
susceptibility tends to one and the scalaron source susceptibility tends to
zero.  These are stationary response readouts, not a new physical-time law.
A substitution $s=s(t)$ requires a controlled quasistatic limit or the full
inherited time-dependent Green/Schur response.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Apply the cited exact-domain inverse theorem and its terminal corollary.  The
remaining derivative terms in the full trace equation give the stated
stationary-versus-history distinction.
\end{proof}

\label{p9:cor:v190-native-k48-abundance-history-functional}
On the native cDM branch, Paper--VI-A fixes the physical Hilbert owner as the

 R=C_6\alpha^{-Q_D}.
\]
Therefore a numerical cDM density on an occurring branch is obtained by

 \boxed{
 \rho_{\rm cDM,obs}
 =\mathcal R_{\rm cDM}
 [\text{generated history},\text{required state/RG/kinetic data},
  \text{observational slice}].}
\]
Neither the carrier-free balanced response grade $E_{48}$ nor the curvature
coordinate $C_6$ supplies an additional multiplicative abundance coefficient.
Introducing such a coefficient would add a new datum to the native branch.
The optional separately conserved material-current theorem below retains its

On the physical post-K16 particle branch the tensor is evaluated through the

\begin{corollary}[Imported environment-conditioned terminal RAR readout]

 \boxed{g_{\rm obs}^{\,2}=a_0\Lambda_{\rm env}g_b,

 \Lambda_{\rm env}=\sqrt{R_{\rm sel}/C_{56}}.}

\begin{theorem}[Optional material-current matching branch]
\label{p9:thm:conditional-residual-k48-matching}
This theorem is not part of the native cDM derivation.  It applies only if a
stronger separately conserved material-current interpretation is independently
imposed.  Let \(t_*\) be a proposed same-action handoff surface and distinguish:
\begin{description}
\item[\(\mathrm{K48MATERIAL}\):] a separately conserved positive
material-current Hilbert action is independently generated for the final-octet current;
\item[\(\mathrm{K48MATCH}\):] the pre-handoff residual geometric stress and
the post-handoff material current are two limits of one conserved same-action
stress tensor.
\end{description}
with no distributional external source/loss at \(t_*\), and with \(H\),
pressure, and the regular source terms bounded there.  Then
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:residual-k48-density-matching}
 \boxed{\rho_{\rm cDM}(t_*^+)
 =\rho_{\rm res}^{\rm geom}(t_*^-).}
\end{equation}
After the handoff, dust conservation therefore fixes
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:k48-normalization-from-matching}
 \rho_{\rm cDM}(a)
 =\rho_{\rm res}^{\rm geom}(t_*^-)
   \left(\frac{a_*}{a}\right)^3.
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:k48-occ-match-norm-implication}
 \boxed{\begin{gathered}
 \mathrm{K48MATERIAL}+\mathrm{K48MATCH}\\
 \Longrightarrow\mathrm{optional\ material\ K48\ stress/abundance\ normalization}.
 \end{gathered}}
\end{equation}
Thus the dimensionful physical normalization is not an independent primitive
on the matched branch.  This material-current comparison is not used to normalize the native
curvature-fluid cDM response, whose Hilbert owner is already the co-realized
homogeneous $H$ modality of Paper~VI-A.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Finite corridor energy versus physical Hilbert deposition]
The preceding single-surface matching theorem is a limiting special case.
On the generated final primitive coordinate \(q:=Q_D\in[0,1]\), the native
finite carrier bookkeeping is already
\[
 \boxed{M_D^{\rm car}(q)=\varepsilon_{\rm car}q.}
\]
A physical Hilbert descent may be written more generally as
\[
 M_D^{\rm phys}(q)=\int_0^q\varepsilon_{\rm phys}(s)\,ds,
 \qquad M_D^{\rm phys}:=2\pi^2a^3\rho_D,
\]
but any difference \(\varepsilon_{\rm phys}-\varepsilon_{\rm car}\) must be
derived by the same-action metric/Hilbert theorem rather than inserted as a
new K48 coefficient.  Thus \(\mathrm{K48GEOM}\) fixes the corridor shape,
\(\mathrm{K48ENERGY}_{\rm finite}\) fixes its finite energy, and
the optional \(\mathrm{K48MATERIAL}\) branch plus actual history controls
only the stronger separately conserved material-current deposition.  The native
curvature-fluid owner requires no such extra premise.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}
In a local homogeneous chart the same-action energy ledger has distributional
form
\[
 \dot\rho+3H\left(\rho+\frac{p}{c^2}\right)=S_{\rm reg},
\]
where \(S_{\rm reg}\) has no delta mass at \(t_*\) by
\((\mathrm{K48MATCH})\).  Integrating from \(t_*-\varepsilon\) to
\(t_*+\varepsilon\), the bounded Hubble, pressure, and regular-source terms
vanish as \(\varepsilon\to0\).  The remaining boundary term is
\(\rho(t_*^+)-\rho(t_*^-)=0\), proving
\eqref{p9:eq:residual-k48-density-matching}.  The post-handoff equation
\(\dot\rho_{\rm cDM}+3H\rho_{\rm cDM}=0\) integrates to
\eqref{p9:eq:k48-normalization-from-matching}.  If either premise is absent,
the boundary value is not supplied, reproducing the counterfamily of the
cited proposition.
\end{proof}

For historical comparison, the following earlier readout convention is retained only as an explicitly non-native conditional member:
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:present-curvature-tick-selector}
\frac{{}^{(3)}R_0}{{}^{(3)}R_{\rm acc}}=e^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Since \({}^{(3)}R\propto a^{-2}\), this is exactly
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:present-deltaN-half}
\Delta N:=\log(a_0/a_{\rm acc})=\frac12.
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Exact late matter/vacuum ratio and physical densities]
\label{p9:thm:late-density-ratio-repaired}
On the dust+vacuum reduction and optional convention
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:present-deltaN-half},
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:r0-exact}
r_0:=\frac{\rho_{m,0}}{\rho_\Lambda}=2e^{-3/2}.
\end{equation}
With the Paper--VI-A terminal constant
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:lambdaeff-native}
\Lambda_{\rm eff}=\frac{C_{56}}2
=\frac{U\alpha^{-7}}2
=\frac{U\alpha^{-7}}2,
\end{equation}
define \(H_{100}=100\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}\).  Then the physical
density coordinates are independent of the still-free curvature depth:
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:physical-density-native}
\boxed{\omega_\Lambda(1)=
\frac{\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2}{3H_{100}^2}
=1\,(0.3663570002\ldots),}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:physical-matter-native}
\boxed{\omega_m(1)=r_0\omega_\Lambda(1)
=1\,(0.1634905922\ldots).}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Dust conservation from the acceleration slice to the selected present slice
gives \(\rho_{m,0}=2\rho_\Lambda e^{-3\Delta N}\); insert
\(\Delta N=1/2\).  The definition of \(\omega_i=\rho_i/\rho_{c,100}\) with
\(\rho_{c,100}=3H_{100}^2/(8\pi G)\) and
\(\rho_\Lambda=\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2/(8\pi G)\) gives
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:physical-density-native}; multiplication by \(r_0\) gives
the matter result.  No \(D_4\) density role or finite-overlap optical factor
enters.
\end{proof}

% BEGIN FRAMEWORK v3.036: LATE-COSMOLOGY PHYSICAL NEWTON OWNER
\begin{remark}[Planck-curvature scale, terminal parameter and physical response]
\label{p9:rem:v3036-cosmology-newton-dependency}
The terminal constant $\Lambda_{\rm eff}=C_{56}/2$ and the numerical
coordinate values $\omega_\Lambda(1)$ and $\omega_m(1)$ above are
preserved as \emph{scalar-normalized comparison outputs}. The primitive
curvature is $U=1/(2\pi\ell_{\rm scal}^2)$, whereas the ordinary joint
heat coefficient is $96$ times its primitive Einstein coefficient.
Physical interpretation of a fixed metrological Planck length,
$G_N$, and the canonically sourced Friedmann/Poisson/RAR observables
requires the complete-action owner condition of
Proposition~\ref{p8:prop:v3036-newton-physical-matching-criterion}.
Cancellation of $G$ inside the dimensionless density ratio does not
prove this physical identification. No new factor of $2\pi$ or $96$ is
inserted into the existing conditional readout formulae.

The affine terminal action $f_{\rm term}(R_g)=R_g-C_{56}$ has the
formal constant-curvature Einstein root $R_g=2C_{56}$, outside its
specified domain $R_g\le C_{56}$.  The adjoining active metric-$f(R)$
source-free root of
Theorem~\ref{p3f:thm:v346-near-terminal-active-vacuum} instead satisfies
$2C_{56}<R_*<3C_{56}$ and
$R_*=2C_{56}+O(C_{56}^2/U)$.
Thus $C_{56}/2$ remains the affine action's cosmological coefficient,
while $R_*/4$ is the \emph{effective curvature parameter} of the
separately solved active vacuum, not a proof of an actual complete-joint
FLRW history.
\end{remark}
% END FRAMEWORK v3.036

\begin{theorem}[Closed-curvature family with its observation-slice argument retained]
\label{p9:thm:closed-curvature-family-repaired}
On the stated late conserved-dust plus constant-vacuum reduction, fix
an admitted observation offset $d:=\Delta N\ge0$ from the acceleration
slice and write $r_0(d)=2e^{-3d}$. Set
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:xN-def}
 x(N;d)=\frac{\alpha^7 e^{-2N-2d}}{1+r_0(d)}.
\end{equation}
For $0<x(N;d)<1$, the expanding closed member has
\begin{align}
 H_0(N;d)&=H_{0,\rm flat}(d)\sqrt{1-x(N;d)},\\
 \Omega_{k,0}&=-\frac{x(N;d)}{1-x(N;d)},\\
 \Omega_{\Lambda,0}&=\frac1{[1+r_0(d)][1-x(N;d)]},\\
 \Omega_{m,0}&=\frac{r_0(d)}{[1+r_0(d)][1-x(N;d)]},
 \label{p9:eq:closed-family}
\end{align}
where
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:H0-flat-native}
 \boxed{H_{0,\rm flat}(d)=
 \sqrt{\frac{\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2}{3}[1+2e^{-3d}]}.}
\end{equation}
Thus $0<H_0<H_{0,\rm flat}(d)$ on each fixed-$d$ family. The
positivity bound and the stricter connected early expanding-history bound are
\[
 N>N_{\min}(d)=\frac72\log\alpha-d-\frac12\log[1+r_0(d)],
 \qquad N>N_{\rm crit}=\frac72\log\alpha-\frac12\log3.
\]
At the optional $d=1/2$, the former printed $x(N)$ and numerical bounds
are recovered; $N=144$ is one comparison member. Subsequent $x(N)$
notation means a fixed declared $d$, never a generated present slice.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
On this reduction $a_{\rm acc}=a_Pe^N$, $a_0=a_Pe^{N+d}$,
$a_P^2=6/U$, and $\Lambda_{\rm eff}=U\alpha^{-7}/2$.
The closed Friedmann equation gives
$H_0^2=(\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2/3)(1+r_0)-c^2/a_0^2$;
their quotient is precisely $x(N;d)$. Dividing each term by $H_0^2$
gives the density fractions, whose sum including curvature is one.
Solving $x<1$ gives $N_{\min}(d)$. The dust constant is proportional

comparison-family calculation; an actual geometric H-response with
exchange must supply its own evolution before these dust formulas apply.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Closed response ratio, shape weighting and apparent vacuum evolution}
\label{p9:sec:v2205-closed-response-readout}

This section propagates the stress and work derived in Paper~VI-A,
Section~\ref{p8:sec:v2205-homogeneous-boundary-stress}, retaining its
action, homogeneity and source census assumptions. Use $c=\hbar=1$,
$\rho_\Lambda=M^2C_7/2$ and physical curvature radius $a$.
The response is allocated once; the already counted source or curvature
stress may not also be inserted as an independent CDM perturbation source.

\begin{proposition}[Expansion-to-response map on an expanding closed branch]
\label{p9:prop:v2205-redshift-response-map}
At an independently identified observation slice $a_*$, let
$1+z=a_*/a$. With $H_z=dH/dz$ and the complete comparison source known,
the two reconstructed response ports are
\begin{align}
\label{p9:eq:v2205-redshift-response-map}
 \rho_\partial(z)&=3M^2\left[H(z)^2+
                      \frac{(1+z)^2}{a_*^2}\right]
                         -\rho_\Lambda-\rho_s(z),\\
 p_\partial(z)&=M^2\left[2(1+z)H(z)H_z(z)-3H(z)^2
                      -\frac{(1+z)^2}{a_*^2}\right]
                         +\rho_\Lambda-p_s(z).\nonumber
\end{align}
For conserved baryonic rest-energy reference
$\rho_b^{\rm rest}(z)=\rho_{b,*}^{\rm rest}(1+z)^3$, set
\[
 \beta(z)=\frac{\rho_\partial(z)}{\rho_b^{\rm rest}(z)}.
\]
If the response is separately conserved and $\beta>0$, its equation of
state is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v2205-redshift-ratio-pressure}
 \boxed{w_\partial(z)=\frac{1+z}{3\beta(z)}\frac{d\beta}{dz}.}
\end{equation}
These are a target-reconstruction map. A prediction requires the native
action/source solution for $H,a_*,\rho_s,p_s$, rather than fitting
$\beta$ and treating the result as an action-derived coefficient.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use $\dot z=-(1+z)H$ and $K=(1+z)^2/a_*^2$ in
Paper~VI-A, Eq.~\eqref{p8:eq:v2205-closed-response-reconstruction}.
Convert Eq.~\eqref{p8:eq:v2205-conserved-ratio-pressure} to redshift.
The selected observation slice is retained throughout; normalizing a
dimensionless comparison scale factor to one does not remove $a_*$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Mass-weighted shape mixture and interaction work]
\label{p9:prop:v2205-mass-weighted-shape-mixture}
Suppose an actually admitted adiabatic body-energy reduction permits
$E_\partial=\sum_i m_i b_i+E_{\rm cross}$, where $m_i>0$ are the
conserved baryonic rest energies and $b_i$ are the action-derived
dimensionless response energies per body. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v2205-mass-weighted-shape-mixture}
 \boxed{\beta=\sum_i\frac{m_i}{M_b}b_i+
                \frac{E_{\rm cross}}{M_b},\qquad M_b=\sum_i m_i.}
\end{equation}
The independent data held fixed in a scale variation are the same as
those of the actual energy reduction. Its pressure is
\[
 p_\partial=-\frac1{3V}
 \left[\sum_i m_i\frac{\partial b_i}{\partial\log a}
                  +\frac{\partial E_{\rm cross}}{\partial\log a}\right].
\]
Actual shape/source evolution contributes its separately retained work.
Consequently a morphology mixture is weighted by baryonic rest energy
on this domain, not automatically by object count, occupied volume or
an arithmetic average of surface factors. Overlapping or nonadditive
response retains $E_{\rm cross}$ and its scale derivative. Proposed
coefficients such as $\pi$ or $4\pi$ become abundance coefficients only
after their identification with the normalized action energies $b_i$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Divide the admitted total energy by $M_b$ and apply the two-port energy
work result of Paper~VI-A,
Proposition~\ref{p8:prop:v2205-geometric-energy-work}.
The decomposition itself is a predicate on the actual reduced action;
geometry alone does not prove additivity or remove the cross term.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A fixed vacuum can be assigned an apparent evolution]
\label{p9:prop:v2205-apparent-vacuum-evolution}
Assume the actual allocated response has
$\rho_\partial=\beta(a)\rho_b^{\rm rest}$ and pressure $p_\partial$.
After the same complete comparison source is subtracted, suppose a
flat-background comparison instead subtracts a constant dust ratio
$\beta_f\rho_b^{\rm rest}$. The remainder assigned to dark energy is
exactly
\begin{align}
\label{p9:eq:v2205-apparent-vacuum-evolution}
 \boxed{\rho_{\rm DE}^{\rm fit}
   =\rho_\Lambda+(\beta-\beta_f)\rho_b^{\rm rest}-3M^2a^{-2},}\\
 \boxed{p_{\rm DE}^{\rm fit}
   =-\rho_\Lambda+p_\partial+M^2a^{-2}.}\nonumber
\end{align}
If the actual comparison source and response are separately conserved,
this remainder is also conserved. Where it is positive and $H\ne0$,
\[
 w_{\rm DE}^{\rm fit}=-1-
            \frac13\frac{d\log\rho_{\rm DE}^{\rm fit}}{d\log a}.
\]
Thus response-ratio evolution and omitted spatial curvature can both
enter an apparent dark-energy evolution while $C_7$ and the terminal
vacuum energy remain fixed. This is an exact comparison bookkeeping
identity, not an explanation of an observed trend without the actual
response history and likelihood calculation. A correctly closed
comparison omits only the two explicit curvature-misallocation terms.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write the actual fixed-reference lapse and spatial equations as
$3M^2H^2=\rho_s+\rho_\Lambda+\beta\rho_b^{\rm rest}-3M^2K$
and
$-M^2(2\dot H+3H^2)=p_s-\rho_\Lambda+p_\partial+M^2K$.
Subtract the stated flat dust comparison. Its constant-ratio dust and
the curvature bookkeeping term are separately conserved, so the
assumed source/response conservation implies the remainder's identity.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The remaining observational test.}
The background supplies two scalar mean ports. The linear response
consumes the complete physical second action variation on the same
prepared source/history, including the closed harmonic spectrum,
constraints, metric slip and causal contacts. A background $\beta$ or a
static RAR law alone does not specify that kernel. Testing expansion,
lensing, clustering and early-time gravitational potentials therefore
requires the same generated mean and perturbation response once each.
No shape mixture or epoch-independent ratio is selected by this section.

\subsection{BBN boundary and reduced response}
\label{p9:app:omegak}

\begin{theorem}[Finite BBN response]
\label{p9:thm:finite-bbn-response}
Let \(\rho_{{\rm ord},\rm rel}^{\rm act}(T)\) denote the relativistic
energy density carried by the actually occupied ordered support
\(\overline{\operatorname{Ran}\Gamma_{16}^{\rm ord}}\), and let
\(\rho_{X,\rm rel}^{\rm act}(T)\) be the relativistic part of the optional
retained source/scalaron stress from
Definition~\ref{p9:def:scalaron-stress-energy-split}. Fix an explicitly
matched reference radiation history in the same energy-density and neutrino
convention, with corresponding densities
\(\rho_{{\rm ord},\rm rel}^{\rm ref}\) and
\(\rho_{X,\rm rel}^{\rm ref}\) (the latter is zero only when the reference
has no such component). With \(\rho_{\nu,1}^{\rm ref}(T)>0\) the density of
one reference neutrino species, the \emph{incremental} equivalent-radiation
observable is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:delta-neff-scalaron-stress}
\boxed{
\Delta N_{\rm eff}^{\rm extra}(T)
:=\frac{\bigl(\rho_{{\rm ord},\rm rel}^{\rm act}
                 -\rho_{{\rm ord},\rm rel}^{\rm ref}\bigr)(T)
          +\bigl(\rho_{X,\rm rel}^{\rm act}
                 -\rho_{X,\rm rel}^{\rm ref}\bigr)(T)}
        {\rho_{\nu,1}^{\rm ref}(T)}.}
\end{equation}
For a reference disjoint from both produced populations this reduces to
their positive extra-density sum, but in general a vanishing
\(\Delta N_{\rm eff}^{\rm extra}\) means a vanishing \emph{net reference
subtraction}, not necessarily that either individual actual population is
absent. The incremental radiation content is fixed by the physical
stress--energy and the matched reference, not by the number of abstract
species or the vertex-space dimension. Under the later-rung mass hypothesis
of Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:transported-hot-cold-kinematic-split}, the produced
ordinary relativistic population contributes to the actual ordered density,
regardless of whether it has thermalized.
For small deviations of the expansion/rate history, light-element shifts obey
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:bbn-response-functional}
\Delta Y_i=
\int d\log T\,G_i^{(H)}(T)\Delta_H(T)
+\sum_a\int d\log T\,G_{ia}^{(\lambda)}(T)
\Delta\log\lambda_a(T).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{p9:eq:delta-neff-scalaron-stress} is the reference-subtracted
energy-density definition after the ordered and retained states have been
propagated. It prevents an already-present standard baseline population from
being added a second time as new radiation. A produced positive relativistic
population is not automatically cold merely because its source began in one
coherent field or because the produced state is not yet thermal.  The response
formula is the Fr\'echet linearization of the deterministic BBN reaction
network with respect to its expansion and reaction-rate histories.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[BBN network history as a generated descendant]
\label{p9:cor:bbn-network-history-descendant}
Let the complete BBN abundance vector $Y(T)$ be governed on the claimed
temperature interval by the generated deterministic reaction system
\[
 \frac{dY}{d\log T}=F(T,Y)
\]
with all expansion/rate inputs in $F$ fixed by their actual owners. If $F$ is
continuous in $T$, locally Lipschitz in $Y$ on the admitted region, the
occurring initial vector $Y(T_0)$ is retained, \emph{and the solution exists
and remains inside that region on the claimed temperature interval}, then
the BBN history is the unique solution descendant on that interval by
Paper~III-A Theorem~\ref{p3a:thm:unique-solution-history-descent}.  On a completed
native lineage that vector and the expansion are inherited state/history
readouts; a supplied application may instead state them conditionally.  The
actual nuclear transition/rate map must still be constructed and evaluated
from its QCD/nuclear source owner, with a controlled thermal reduction if the
$T$-network representation is used.  No second network-history selector is
licensed, and network uniqueness alone supplies none of those missing maps
or their domains.
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[Scoped analytic helium freeze-out response]
\label{p9:lem:first-order-bbn-freezeout}
In the simplified weak freeze-out comparison with
$\Gamma_{\rm weak}\propto T^5$ and radiation-era $H\propto T^2$,
a small uniform fractional rescaling $H\mapsto(1+\delta)H$ gives
$\Delta T_f/T_f=\delta/3$. If that rescaling persists through the
decay interval, the comparison time law is
$t(T;\delta)=t(T;0)/(1+\delta)$, and the nuclear reference temperature
is fixed, then
\[
 \Delta\log X_N=
 \left[\frac{\Delta m_{np}}{3T_f}
       +\frac{t_N-5t_f/3}{\tau_n}\right]\delta+O(\delta^2),
\]
and, for the same approximate ratio model
$Y_p=2X_N/(1+X_N)$,
\[
 \Delta Y_p=\frac{Y_p}{1+X_N}
 \left[\frac{\Delta m_{np}}{3T_f}
       +\frac{t_N-5t_f/3}{\tau_n}\right]\delta+O(\delta^2).
\]
The former $(t_N-t_f)/\tau_n$ coefficient neglects the induced shift
of the freeze-out endpoint; its correction is $-2t_f/(3\tau_n)$.
When $t_f\ll t_N$ it scarcely changes the rough diagnostic
$\Delta Y_p\simeq0.17\delta$. A deformation restricted to freeze-out
requires its actual later-time/rate functional instead of this uniform
comparison. Neither form is a precision BBN network prediction.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Logarithmic freeze-out variation gives $5\Delta\log T_f=
2\Delta\log T_f+\delta$. At the shifted temperature,
$t_f(\delta)=t_f(0)(1+\delta)^{-5/3}$, whereas
$t_N(\delta)=t_N(0)(1+\delta)^{-1}$. Differentiate the explicitly
simplified ratio $X_N=\exp[-\Delta m_{np}/T_f-
(t_N-t_f)/\tau_n]$ and then $Y_p$. The complete finite network
uses its actual transition/rate and expansion sources and the positivity
certificate \ref{p6b:prop:v2210-lit-nuclear-network}; no optical
$24/25$ or retired $H_8$ multiplier enters this calculation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Root-state propagation and the source-conditioned readout handoff}
\label{p9:sec:v2109-root-history-handoff}
On the cyclic branch the root geometry and classical history are already
generated.  Once an occurring quantum root state and its same-action
generator are supplied by QROOT, the state at K16 is their evolved
restriction, not an independent \(\mathrm{State}_{16}^{G0}\) selection.
The finite restrictions and Duhamel boundary-flux estimates of
Paper~IV, Theorems~\ref{p4:thm:v2109-finite-root-restrictions} and
\ref{p4:thm:v2109-finite-evolution-error}, make this handoff constructive
on their homogeneous quadratic domain.  RG16, production, kinetic,
thermalization and abundance claims retain precisely the inputs and
analytic estimates they consume.

\begin{proposition}[Root vacuum and instantaneous vacuum history are distinct]
\label{p9:prop:v2109-ground-history}
On a fixed finite carrier let \(H(t)\) be differentiable and self-adjoint,
\(E(t)=H(t)-e_0(t)I\succeq0\), where \(e_0(t)\) is its ground energy.
A density satisfying both
\[
 i\hbar\dot\rho=[H(t),\rho],\qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}(\rho(t)E(t))=0\quad\hbox{for every }t
\]
exists exactly when \(\bigcap_t\ker E(t)\ne\{0\}\).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Positivity puts \(\rho(t)\) in each instantaneous ground space.
On that support \(H(t)\) is scalar, so its commutator vanishes and the
density is constant.  Its support therefore lies in the intersection.
Conversely, a constant density supported in that intersection satisfies
both equations.  A moving representation makes the same statement with
covariant parallel transport, retaining its connection.
\end{proof}
Thus later source evaluation uses the evolved state, not repeated
instantaneous-vacuum preparation.  The native tensor-root check in
Appendix~\ref{p9:app:v2109-outgoing-vacuum-check} supplies an explicit
example on the retained quadratic representative.

\paragraph{Effective RAR readout status.}
The native terminal \(S+T\) deep relation remains closed.  Paper~III-F now
supplies the Hilbert-to-lapse source, its adjoint Poisson projection,
the finite cut inverse, complete one-port passive quadratic elimination,
and the fixed-source Born--Legendre action identity.  Its exact
moving-source Schur remainder retains changing backgrounds, ports and
kinetic metrics.  These are explicit source/response calculations, not a
proof that one scalar baryonic acceleration exhausts the complete galaxy
source or selects a universal global interpolation.  G26-GLOBAL remains
that same-action source-conditioned calculation; it does not reopen
G26-DEEP.

\subsection{The charge computation consumes the complete descendant}

 H_\partial(t)=H_{N,\rm red}(t)+\tfrac12(A_{BL2}(t)+A_{BL2}(t)^\dagger).

no new vacuum is imposed at $t_8$, $t_e$ or $t_{16}$. Starting a calculation
at inflation end retains both
\[

\subsection{The full same-history source and final yield}
\label{p9:v2171:sec:finalyield}
Let $\mathcal E(t)$ denote the actually generated baryon-export source in
the physical regulated charge law. This denotes a missing calculation,
not a free rate to install. On the domain where the only explicit
$B-L$-violating constituent is the owned BL2 pair and the anomalous source
has the equal $B,L$ coefficients just computed, the complete balances are
\begin{equation}
 \dot q_B=\omega_t(\mathcal E),\qquad
 \dot q_L=\omega_t(\mathcal E)+\frac2\hbar\Im\omega_t(A_{BL2}),\qquad
 \dot q_D=-\frac2\hbar\Im\omega_t(A_{BL2}).
 \label{p9:v2171:eq:coupledbalances}
\end{equation}

Let $\mathscr S_{\rm mat}(t)>0$ be the entropy in the same owned material

 Y_D(t_*)=\frac{q_D(t_*)}{\mathscr S_{\rm mat}(t_*)},\qquad

The late physical photon map and event in the last ratio are consumed if

\begin{proposition}[Charge and entropy tail certificate]
\label{p9:v2171:prop:tailcertificate}
Suppose the complete physical charge rate on the continued same-history
domain has an integrable bound $r_X(t)\ge|\dot q_X(t)|$, and a positive
entropy continuation obeys
$\delta_S:=\int_{t_*}^{\infty}|\dot{\mathscr S}_{\rm mat}|dt
 <\mathscr S_*:=\mathscr S_{\rm mat}(t_*)$.
Put $\delta_q=\int_{t_*}^{\infty}r_X(t)dt$.
Then both limits exist and
\begin{equation}
 |Y_X(\infty)-Y_X(t_*)|
 \le \frac{\delta_q}{\mathscr S_*-\delta_S}
 +\frac{|q_X(t_*)|\delta_S}
        {\mathscr S_*(\mathscr S_*-\delta_S)}.
 \label{p9:v2171:eq:tailbound}
\end{equation}
On a complete finite Hamiltonian domain one may take
$r_X=\|\mathcal J_X\|$. No selected freeze-out temperature follows from
this criterion; the actual continued rates and entropy must establish it.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Absolute integrability makes $q_X$ and $\mathscr S$ Cauchy, with tails
bounded by $\delta_q$ and $\delta_S$. Positivity gives
$\mathscr S_\infty\ge\mathscr S_*-\delta_S>0$. Subtract the two ratios
and apply these bounds to the numerator and reciprocal denominator.
\end{proof}

The globally inherited neutral root is pure on its complete closed unitary

\subsection{Charge survival and the same-history yield}
On the stated pair-charge branch the exact inherited Ward law is
\[
 \dot q(t)=-\frac2\hbar\operatorname{Im}\langle A_{BL2}(t)\rangle,
 \qquad q(t)=\langle Q_{B-L}\rangle_t.
\]
The complete evolved expectation is calculated by
Theorem~\ref{p4:v2171:thm:flow}, or the existing quadratic theorem on its domain.

\begin{proposition}[Sufficient late-charge endpoint control]\label{p9:v2171:prop:tail}
If the same finite history continues to infinity and
\[
 \int_{t_f}^{\infty}\|[H(t),Q]\|_{\rm op}\,dt<\infty,
\]
then $q(t)$ has a finite limit and
\begin{equation}\label{p9:v2171:eq:tail}
 |q(\infty)-q(t_f)|\leq\hbar^{-1}
  \int_{t_f}^{\infty}\|[H(t),Q]\|_{\rm op}\,dt.
\end{equation}
In particular, if the actual late owner commutes with $Q$, the charge is
exactly frozen from that time. If the actual comoving material entropy in
the same physical volume satisfies $S(t)\to S_\infty>0$, then
$Y_{B-L}(t)=q(t)/S(t)$ tends to $q(\infty)/S_\infty$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Normalized density matrices obey
$|\Tr\rho\,i[H,Q]|\leq\|[H,Q]\|_{\rm op}$. Integrate the exact source
law to bound every difference $q(t_2)-q(t_1)$. The integrable tail is a
Cauchy criterion and gives \eqref{p9:v2171:eq:tail}. The ratio limit follows from
$S_\infty>0$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Native endpoint scope.}
This is a sufficient analytic certificate, not an assumed native washout
profile. The material entropy is an actual same-history thermodynamic
readout; it is not identified with the conserved global von Neumann entropy
of a closed finite state. Native collisions, washout, spectator redistribution,

must be generated/evaluated on the same history. Their final ratio has no
independent efficiency parameter. A time-averaged finite charge, a local CP

put \(r=\log(a\sqrt F)\).  The instantaneous annihilator obeys
\[
 \dot b=-i\omega b+\dot r\,b^\dagger,\qquad \omega=\nu/a.
\]
The same-action classical root jets give
\[
 \dot r_0=0,\qquad
 \ddot r_0=\dot H_0+\frac{\ddot F_0}{2F_*}
 =\frac{R_*-U}{6}+\frac{2U-R_*}{6}=\frac U6>0.
\]
Starting from its root ground, integrating the linear Bogoliubov equation
therefore gives
\[
 \beta(t)=\frac{U}{12}t^2+O(t^3),\qquad
 n(t)=\frac{U^2}{144}t^4+O(t^5).
\]
A finite compression admitting the two-particle sector retains this leading
pair production; a cap excluding that sector is a different domain.
This calculation does not prove the physical root state occurs.  It shows
why, even after root selection, a continuously reset instantaneous vacuum
would change the inherited quantum history.  Later quantum-source
accounting must keep the evolved tensor covariance, pressure and scalaron
response, including the source-work term on a varying \(F\) background.
Dilution of energy as a gap closes is not by itself exhaustion of quantum
occupation or parent records.

\subsection{Cosmic Curvature Fluid, RAR, and the Separate Reheating Portal}
\label{p9:sec:portal-closed}
The dark sector contains no particle-DM branch.  Its static component is the
Paper--VI-A RAR kernel, and its homogeneous component is the conditional
effective curvature fluid at \(j_{\rm cDM}=6, K_{\rm cDM}=48\).  Separately,
scalaron excitations and their literal RG/Weyl coupling to the Standard Model
may be used in inflationary reheating.  The next portal calculation belongs to
that reheating interface only; it does not create a dark-matter particle,
abundance, or halo mass.

\begin{definition}[Ordered realization and retained source geometry]
\label{p9:def:ordered-realization-retained-geometry}
Import Paper~VI-A's ordered quadratic kernel and type-correct pair vertex
\[
 K_{\rm ord}
 =\left.\frac{\delta^2S_{\rm tot}}
 {\delta\Psi_{\rm ord}\,\delta\Psi_{\rm ord}}
 \right|_{\Psi_{\rm ord}=0},
 \qquad
 V_{16}:\mathcal H_1\longrightarrow\mathcal V_{\rm pair},
 \qquad
 V_{16}(\eta)=\left.\nabla_\eta K_{\rm ord}\right|_{C_2}.
\]
Define the source-side decomposition and the actually occupied ordered-mode
support by
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:vertex-source-and-occupation-support}
 \mathcal H_{\rm src}^{\rm tr}:=(\ker V_{16})^\perp,
 \qquad
 \mathcal H_{\rm src}^{\rm ret}:=\ker V_{16},
 \qquad
 \mathcal H_{\rm out}^{\rm ord}
 :=\overline{\operatorname{Ran}\Gamma_{16}^{\rm ord}}
 \subseteq\mathcal H_{\rm ord}.
\end{equation}
The ordered realization is the positive produced stress/energy on
\(\mathcal H_{\rm out}^{\rm ord}\) obtained from Paper~IV's production
density \(\Gamma_{16}^{\rm ord}\).  The retained geometry is the coherent
source stress supported on \(\mathcal H_{\rm src}^{\rm ret}\) and propagated
by the same-action ledger.  On the principal equivariant target these are
\(\mathcal H_{\rm src}^{\rm tr}=\mathcal E_9\) and
\(\mathcal H_{\rm src}^{\rm ret}=\mathcal L_6\).

The range \(\operatorname{Ran}V_{16}\subseteq\mathcal V_{\rm pair}\) is a
quadratic/Nambu vertex space.  It is not a one-particle output space and is
not identified with \(\mathcal H_{\rm out}^{\rm ord}\).  For an action even
in \(\Psi_{\rm ord}\), the former mixed second derivative with only one
matter variation vanishes at \(\Psi_{\rm ord}=0\); it cannot replace
\(V_{16}\).

This decomposition is a reheating/BBN interface.  Optional scalaron
excitations and thermal portal terms may redistribute energy after production,
but neither is identified with the later effective \(K=48\) dust current.
Such an identification would require the separate occurrence and matching
theorem stated below.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[RG matching derivative and scalaron normalization]
\label{p9:def:portal-matching-data}
At a declared thermal matching scale \(\mu_*\), let
\[
D:=\mu\frac{d}{d\mu},\qquad \beta_y:=Dy_t.
\]
Paper~VI-A uses the dimensionless Einstein-frame scalaron coordinate
\(\varphi=\sqrt{3/2}\log F\).  Write the canonically dimension-one field as
\[
\phi_c:=\bar M_{\rm Pl}\varphi,
\qquad
\tau:=\alpha^{-1/2}\varphi
      =\alpha^{-1/2}\frac{\phi_c}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}}.
\]
This is the declared breathing/RG source.  It is distinct from the
geometric metric Weyl coordinate
\[
 \sigma=\tfrac12\log F=\frac{\phi_c}{\sqrt6\bar M_{\rm Pl}},
 \qquad \tau=\sqrt6\alpha^{-1/2}\sigma.
\]
Paper~V-C, Remark~\ref{p7:rem:v2216-weyl-source-map}, shows that the
metric Weyl transformation alone supplies $\sigma$.  Physical use of
the $\tau$ portal requires the actual breathing-source/action pullback;
its hierarchy factor is not derived by canonical scalaron normalization.
All portal coefficients below are coefficients with respect to this
declared $\tau$ source until that incidence is supplied.  If the claim
instead concerns the purely geometric Weyl anomaly, the linear source
factor is $1/\sqrt6$ and the quadratic factor is $1/6$ in place of
$\alpha^{-1/2}$ and $\alpha^{-1}$, respectively.  These are distinct
typed branches, not a choice of transfer efficiency.
On the optional smooth-MRESP comparison, conditional on Paper~VI-A's
\((\mathrm{MASS}_{\rm UV})\) identification and its displayed
continuum-decay and thermal interfaces, the unsplit reheating branch has
\(T_{\rm reh}^{(0,E)}=2.9588\times10^9\,\mathrm{GeV}\).  Alternatively,
Paper~VI-A's direct hierarchy branch supplies
\[
 T_{16}^{E}(\xi_{16}^{\rm dyn})
 =5.0457050\times10^9\,(\xi_{16}^{\rm dyn})^{1/4}\ {\rm GeV}
\]
on the generated \(\mathrm{REG}_0\) branch, but only under
\((\mathrm{REH}_{16}[\xi])+\mathrm{THERM}\).  In particular,
\(\xi_{16}^{\rm dyn}=\delta_{\rm geom}:=1/\sqrt{72}\) gives
\(T_{16}^{E,\delta}=2.9563463\times10^9\,\mathrm{GeV}\) as a conditional
diagnostic, not as a count-derived coefficient.  No Paper--VI-B readout may
identify these two branches or select either missing interface.  A
first-split temperature may be used only on the explicit Paper--VI-A
\(\mathrm{DEM}_{15}+\mathrm{Hkin}\) branch; its \(3/5\) is a support
availability and its actual energy fraction is
\((3/5)\varepsilon_{\rm dep}=\xi_{16}^{\rm dyn}\).  Neither quantity is an
unconditional Paper--VI-B input.  None of these comparison temperatures is
imported as an output of the active \(n_2^+/n_7^+\) action.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Exact first and second RG/Weyl coefficients of the thermal top term]
\label{p9:lem:two-higgs-anomaly-matching}
Hold the thermal scale \(T\) fixed while taking the RG derivative.  For the
unbroken high-temperature quadratic top determinant
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:top-thermal-det-quadratic}
\Gamma_t^{(2)}
=\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\;A_t(T)y_t^2H^\dagger H,
\qquad A_t(T)=\frac{N_cT^2}{12},
\end{equation}
the Weyl/RG spurion \(\mu\mapsto\mu e^{-\tau}\) gives
\begin{align}
\Gamma_t^{(2)}(\mu e^{-\tau})
={}&\Gamma_t^{(2)}(\mu)
-2A_t y_t\beta_y\,\tau\,H^\dagger H \notag\\
&+A_t\bigl(\beta_y^2+y_tD\beta_y\bigr)\tau^2H^\dagger H
+O(\tau^3),
\label{p9:eq:literal-rg-weyl-expansion}
\end{align}
inside the local density.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The spurion acts by \(e^{-\tau D}\).  Since
\[
D(y_t^2)=2y_t\beta_y,
\qquad
D^2(y_t^2)=2\bigl(\beta_y^2+y_tD\beta_y\bigr),
\]
Taylor expansion
\(e^{-\tau D}=1-\tau D+\tfrac12\tau^2D^2+O(\tau^3)\)
gives Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:literal-rg-weyl-expansion}.  No additional quadratic
matching prescription is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Thermal-portal chronology]
\label{p9:rem:thermal-portal-chronology}
Because \(A_t(T)=N_cT^2/12\), the thermal portal vanishes at \(T=0\).  It
therefore cannot seed the first particles or the first radiation bath from an
exact vacuum.  Its chronological role is feedback or redistribution after a
nonzero state/temperature has already been produced.  The available seed
mechanisms are the nonadiabatic Bogoliubov production of Paper~IV and the
ordered-kernel pair vertex \(V_{16}\).  The thermal top term below is one
post-seed realization of the scalar-to-Higgs block of \(V_{16}\), not the
first-bath source.  A zero-temperature ordinary gauge-pair block is already generated by
Paper~V-C and imported below.  What remains history-dependent is nonzero
occupation/deposition, backreaction, and kinetic convergence; adiabatic or
interference zeros are not excluded by vertex nonvanishing alone.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Literal anomaly/RG scalaron--Higgs interaction]
\label{p9:thm:closed-anomaly-portal}
On the stated RG and thermal interface, the canonically normalized scalaron
interaction through second order is
\begin{align}
\Delta\mathcal L_{\phi H}^{(1)}
&=-\frac{2\alpha^{-1/2}A_t(T)y_t\beta_y}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}}
  \phi_c H^\dagger H,\\
\Delta\mathcal L_{\phi H}^{(2)}
&=\frac{\alpha^{-1}A_t(T)}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}^{2}}
  \bigl(\beta_y^2+y_tD\beta_y\bigr)
  \phi_c^2H^\dagger H.
\label{p9:eq:closed-portal}
\end{align}
Thus the former constant reduced coefficient
\(\alpha^{-1}\beta_y/(2y_t)\) is not the literal second RG/Weyl derivative.
In particular, a quadratic-only production model requires an independently
typed even-sector or process projection; it is not generated by the anomaly
identity alone.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Substitute
\(\tau=\alpha^{-1/2}\phi_c/\bar M_{\rm Pl}\) into
Lemma~\ref{p9:lem:two-higgs-anomaly-matching}.  The coefficient of
\(\phi_c^2H^\dagger H\) is dimensionless because \(A_t\) has mass dimension
two.  The displayed linear term is part of the same expansion and therefore
cannot be discarded without an additional symmetry/process statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[RGE closure and hypercharge convention]
At one loop, keeping the displayed top-Yukawa-only contribution,
\[
\frac{\beta_{y_t}}{y_t}
=\frac{1}{16\pi^2}
\left[\frac92y_t^2-\frac{17}{20}g_1^2-\frac94g_2^2-8g_3^2\right]+\cdots,
\qquad g_1^2=\frac53g_Y^2.
\]
Here \(g_1\) is the GUT-normalized hypercharge coupling of Paper~V-C,
Eq.~\eqref{p7v2:eq:g1-convention}. Equivalently the hypercharge contribution
is \(-17g_Y^2/12\). The ellipsis retains the omitted bottom-Yukawa,
lepton-Yukawa, and higher-order terms when applicable. The former expression
\(-17g_1/12\) was neither quadratic in the gauge coupling nor correct in
this normalization. The quantity \(D\beta_y\) is obtained by differentiating
the same declared RGE system, including the running of all couplings appearing
in \(\beta_y\); this does not introduce a free portal parameter.
\end{remark}


\begin{corollary}[Zero-temperature first-bath gauge seed]
\label{p9:cor:v180-k16-zeroT-gauge-seed}
Let $e_0\in\Lambda^0_{\mathbb C}\subset\mathcal E_9$ be the local scalaron
source direction at the generated $K=16$ event.  Paper~V-C,
Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:v164-scalar-gauge-source}, gives
\[
 \Delta\mathcal L_{\phi FF}^{(1)}(\mu)
 =-\frac{\alpha^{-1/2}\phi_c}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}}
 \sum_i\frac{b_i g_i^2(\mu)}{32\pi^2}F_{i,c}^{\,2}
 +O(g_i^4(\mu)\tau).
\]
At the source-native common-heat boundary \(\mu_h\), the three one-loop gauge
weights are
\[
 \boxed{\left(\frac{41}{160},-\frac{19}{96},-\frac7{16}\right)},
\]
so each displayed gauge block is nonzero at zero temperature.  Hence
\[
 \boxed{V_{16}^{A_iA_i}(e_0)\neq0}
\]
for the constituted gauge-pair target blocks on the stated branch.  Therefore
a first ordinary $K16$ pair-production source exists without a pre-existing
thermal bath.

This closes source/vertex existence on the declared breathing-source
branch.  Its physical scalaron identification retains
Definition~\ref{p9:def:portal-matching-data}'s source pullback.
The actual occupation is history
dependent and can still vanish on stationary, adiabatic, or cancelling
histories; on the restored post-K16 local-WKB branch its generated evolution is

the closed basic seed statement by
\[
 \boxed{\mathrm{VERTSEED16}:\exists i,\;V_{16}^{A_iA_i}(e_0)\neq0},
 \qquad \mathrm{VERTSEED16}=\texttt{CLOSED}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Finite-pass production and inherited-state readouts]
\label{p9:rem:v2216-gauge-variation-import}
For an actual positive gauge profile $Z_i$,
