% Exact chart correction for VI-B p9:thm:v181-kin16-bogoliubov-backreaction.
% This fragment is a downstream physical application of an elementary
% differentiation identity. No I-A/I-B theorem consumes it.
% Source Paper VI-B, original line 1040
\subsubsection{The proper-time mass chart at the coefficient freeze}
\label{p9:sec:v215-mass-chart}
Write $M=\bar M_{\rm Pl}$, $U=4M^2$, $\chi=f_{RR}$,
$F=f_R>0$, $P=RF-f$ and $B=2f-RF$. On the regular Einstein chart,
\[
 \phi=M\sqrt{3/2}\log F,\qquad dt_E=\sqrt F\,dt_J,
 \qquad V_E(\phi)=\frac{M^2P}{2F^2}.
\]
The canonical field here is Paper VI-B's $\phi_c$ on the common
matched Einstein branch. The Jordan trace stiffness and the canonical
Einstein potential Hessian are respectively
\[
 m_J^2:=\frac{F-R\chi}{3\chi},\qquad
 \mathcal E:=V_{E,\phi\phi}
 =\frac{m_J^2}{F}-\frac{2B}{3F^2}
 =\frac1{3\chi}+\frac R{3F}-\frac{4f}{3F^2}.
\tag{MC.1}\label{p9:eq:v215-einstein-mass-chart}
\]
Thus even at a constant-curvature stationary point the relation is
$\mathcal E=m_J^2/F$; away from stationarity the $B$ term is retained.
For $\mathcal E>0$ put $m_E=\sqrt{\mathcal E}$. Exact differentiation
on each smooth action stratum gives
\[
 \mathcal E_R=-\frac{\chi_R}{3\chi^2}
       -\frac{F-R\chi}{F^2}+\frac{4B\chi}{3F^3},\qquad
 \boxed{\epsilon_{m,E}:=\frac{|\dot m_E|}{m_E^2}
 =\frac{|\mathcal E_R\dot R_J|}
              {2\sqrt F\,\mathcal E^{3/2}}.}
\tag{MC.2}\label{p9:eq:v215-einstein-mass-adiabaticity}
\]
Dots without the subscript $J$ in an Einstein-frame pump calculation
refer to $t_E$. In particular the very small derivative of the Jordan
trace stiffness is not, by itself, a bound on this quantity.

\begin{proposition}[One-sided post-freeze bounds from the actual root]
\label{p9:prop:v215-post-freeze-chart-bounds}
Use the actual cyclic-root energy ceiling of
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:v215-actual-root-energy},
$\rho_0\le c_0M^4/Q$, where
\[
 Q=8\sqrt\alpha,\quad
 c_0=\frac{2(Q\alpha^{-7}+16\pi^4)}
              {[1+1.97901(4\pi^2)]^2}<\frac12.
\]
Assume the expanding regular canonical Einstein lineage has no external
energy injection and has nonnegative $\rho+p$ and nonnegative component
energies. Then $\rho_{\rm tot}\le\rho_0$. On the restored lower action
stratum $C_7<R\le C_2$, the bare gravitational potential Hessian obeys
\[
 \mathcal E\ge\frac U6(1-6\alpha^{-2})>0.
\]
Consequently closed Friedmann gives
\[
 \frac{|H_E|}{m_E}\le
  \sqrt{\frac{c_0}{2Q(1-6\alpha^{-2})}}<1.3\times10^{-5},
 \qquad
 a_Em_E\ge\sqrt{\frac{2Q(1-6\alpha^{-2})}{c_0}}.
\tag{MC.3}\label{p9:eq:v215-canonical-energy-scale-bounds}
\]
At the one-sided post-freeze point $R=C_2^-$, the native secant
functions additionally give the conservative analytic estimate
\[
 \boxed{\epsilon_{m,E}\le
 \frac{6(1+2\alpha^{-2})}{(1-6\alpha^{-2})^{3/2}}
          \sqrt{\frac{2c_0}{Q}}<1.1\times10^{-4}.}
\tag{MC.4}\label{p9:eq:v215-post-freeze-mass-slow}
\]
These scale and derivative bounds do not establish an oscillatory
coherent scalaron phase, its residence time, a populated gauge window,
or a nonzero production history. Additional matter-dependent terms in
the complete quadratic scalar kernel remain part of that actual kernel.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Canonical total energy conservation on the stated expanding branch gives
$\dot\rho_{\rm tot}=-3H_E(\rho+p)\le0$. The restored lower action has
$0<\chi\le2/U$, $F\ge1$, $P\ge0$, and hence $f\le RF$.
Equation~\eqref{p9:eq:v215-einstein-mass-chart} therefore yields
\[
 \mathcal E\ge\frac U6-\frac R F
             \ge\frac U6-R
             \ge\frac U6(1-6\alpha^{-2}).
\]
Use $H_E^2+a_E^{-2}=\rho_{\rm tot}/(3M^2)$ and $U=4M^2$
to obtain both inequalities in
\eqref{p9:eq:v215-canonical-energy-scale-bounds}.

For the one-sided derivative estimate put $L=\log\alpha$ and
$d=\log(1+\alpha^{-3})-\log(1+\alpha^{-7})$. The native rank-seven
secant at $C_2$ is exactly
\[
 n_7=1-\frac d{2L},\qquad
 (n_7)_R=\frac{1/(1+\alpha^3)-d/(4L)}{LC_2},\qquad
 \chi=\frac{(1+n_7)(1-C_7^2/R^2)}{U+C_7}.
\]
Here $(n_7)_R\ge0$ by monotonicity. Since $0<d<\alpha^{-3}$,
for the native $\alpha>10$ these formulas give
\[
 \frac1U\le\chi\le\frac2U,\qquad
 0\le\chi_R\le
 \frac{\alpha^{-1}/L+4\alpha^{-8}}{U^2}<\frac1{U^2}
 \quad(R=C_2^-).
\]
Terminal normalization also gives $F\le1+2\alpha^{-2}$ and
$f\ge R-C_7$. Thus $0<B\le RF$ at $C_2$, because
$B\ge U\alpha^{-2}(1-2\alpha^{-2}-2\alpha^{-5})>0$.
Equation~\eqref{p9:eq:v215-einstein-mass-adiabaticity} now gives
\[
 |\mathcal E_R|\le\frac13+1+\frac83\alpha^{-2}<2.
\]
Finally $\dot R_E=\sqrt{2/3}F\dot\phi/(M\chi)$ and positivity of
component energies gives
$|\dot\phi|\le\sqrt{2\rho_{\rm tot}}
\le M^2\sqrt{2c_0/Q}$. Substitution, using $1/\chi\le U$ and
$\mathcal E\ge U(1-6\alpha^{-2})/6$, proves
\eqref{p9:eq:v215-post-freeze-mass-slow}. The numerical inequalities
use only the existing exact $\alpha=\exp(4\pi^2+1/(6\sqrt2))$.
Every derivative is one-sided on the smooth post-freeze stratum; no
claim transports it across the third-jet kink or to a later descent.
\end{proof}

The chain rule in
Proposition~\ref{p1b:prop:v215-log-legendre-hessian}, with
$a=M\sqrt{3/2}$ and $b=M^2/2$, proves the mass-chart identities.
The field normalization, proper-time chart and total energy conditions
are the physical inputs retained in this application.

\begin{theorem}[KIN16 local WKB Bogoliubov production on the restored scalaron branch]
\label{p9:thm:v181-kin16-bogoliubov-backreaction}
Let the actual post-K16 history lie on a nonzero interval on which the restored
same-action canonical Einstein scalaron admits
\[
 \phi_c(t)=\Phi(t)\cos\Theta(t)+O(\varepsilon_{\rm WKB}),
 \qquad \dot\Theta=m_s(t),
\]
with $t=t_E$ and $m_s$ its actual canonical phase frequency,
\[
 \epsilon_H:=H_E/m_s\ll1,\qquad
 \epsilon_m:=|\dot m_s|/m_s^2\ll1,\qquad
 \epsilon_{\lambda,i}:=|\lambda_i(\mu)\Phi|\ll1.
\]
The local rate additionally uses an admitted many-shell/sweep and phase-coarse-graining domain, as made explicit in Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:v215-pulse-rate-order}. Let
\[
 Z_i(\phi_c;\mu)=1+\lambda_i(\mu)\phi_c+O(\phi_c^2),\qquad
 \lambda_i(\mu)=\frac{\alpha^{-1/2}}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}}
 \frac{b_i g_i^2(\mu)}{8\pi^2}.
\]
For each transverse closed-$S^3$ gauge oscillator,
\[
 v_{i,n}''+\left[n^2-\frac{(\sqrt{Z_i})''}{\sqrt{Z_i}}\right]v_{i,n}=0.
\]
The Paper~IV nonstationary-oscillator equations therefore determine its
occupation/coherence pair.  Leading stationary phase is transverse at
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v186-running-mass-resonance}
 \boxed{2n=a(t_*)m_s(t_*).}
\end{equation}
The sweep derivative which enters the one-mode stationary-phase denominator
also enters the density of modes crossing resonance per unit time and cancels
at leading order.  Hence the local production kernel retains the same form
with the instantaneous mass,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v186-running-mass-production-kernel}
 \boxed{
 Q_g(t)=\Gamma_g(t;\mu)\rho_{\rm coh}(t)
 \left(1-\frac4{a^2m_s^2}\right)
 [1+O(\varepsilon_{\rm WKB})],}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v186-running-mass-gamma}
 \boxed{
 \Gamma_g(t;\mu)=\frac{m_s(t)^3}{64\pi}
 \sum_i d_i\lambda_i^2(\mu).}
\end{equation}
Here $\rho_{\rm coh}$ is the locally coherent scalaron energy entering the
WKB pump.  Equation~\eqref{p9:eq:v186-running-mass-production-kernel} is a
local source term for the exact same-action background equations; it does not
replace the variable-susceptibility scalaron law by a globally constant-mass
two-fluid model.  In particular no primitive positive damping coefficient is
inserted.

On the expanding canonical positive-energy lineage of
Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:v215-post-freeze-chart-bounds}, the local
potential-Hessian comparison uses $m_s=m_E=\sqrt{V_{E,\phi\phi}}$,
rather than the unconverted Jordan trace stiffness. Its scale and
one-sided post-freeze derivative bounds are
\[
 \frac{|H_E|}{m_E}<1.3\times10^{-5},\qquad
 \left.\frac{|\dot m_E|}{m_E^2}\right|_{C_2^-}<1.1\times10^{-4}.
\]
The same root energy bound, on an actually admitted local quadratic
coherent branch with $\rho_{\rm coh}\ge m_E^2\Phi^2/2$, gives
\[
 \frac{\Phi}{M}<\sqrt{\frac3Q},\qquad
 |\lambda_i\Phi|<7.8\times10^{-14}
              \left|\frac{b_i g_i^2}{8\pi^2}\right|,
 \qquad M=\bar M_{\rm Pl},\quad Q=8\sqrt\alpha.
\]
These are scale and local mass-drift certificates on that stated
domain. They do not establish the coherent phase assumed at the start
of this theorem or its residence time. Accordingly
\[
 \boxed{\mathrm{SCALE}^{\rm canonical}_{16,\rm cyc}
  =\texttt{DERIVED\_ON\_EXPANDING\_ENERGY\_DOMAIN}.}
\]
The full local-WKB production claim retains its actual coherent phase,
complete canonical quadratic kernel, physical $g_i(\mu)$, admitted
mode band, sweep and coarse-graining conditions. A small derivative
of the Jordan trace frequency is not a canonical Einstein-time
certificate and is not extended through the coefficient-freeze kink.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The mode equation follows exactly from canonical normalization of the gauge
oscillator.  Expanding to first order in $\lambda_i\Phi$, the resonant phase is
$2n\eta-\Theta$ and its stationary point is
$2n=a m_s$.  The leading stationary-phase probability is inversely
proportional to the absolute sweep of $a m_s$; the number of coexact modes
entering resonance per unit time is proportional to the same sweep, so their
product is sweep-independent to leading order.  The remaining degeneracy and
normalization algebra is the same as in the constant-mass specialization and
gives the displayed instantaneous kernel.  The WKB errors acquire the new
$\epsilon_m$ contribution.

For the scale bounds use the actual root
$R_0/U=4\pi^2/Q$, not an above-$U$ comparison root.
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:v215-actual-root-energy} gives
$\rho_0\le c_0M^4/Q$ with $c_0<1/2$. The canonical mass and
proper-time calculation is
Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:v215-post-freeze-chart-bounds}; its proof
retains the $F$ and off-stationary $B=2f-RF$ terms.
Combining its lower Hessian bound with the stipulated coherent energy
inequality proves the amplitude estimate (the stronger $c_0<1/2$
absorbs the displayed $\alpha^{-2}$ correction). Continuity gives
persistence of strict local inequalities on a one-sided regular
interval. It supplies neither an oscillatory phase nor a residence
interval of any specified duration.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Constant-mass homogenized KIN16 comparison constants]
\label{p9:cor:v181-kin16-universal-equality}
If, on a further interval, $m_s$ and the running couplings may be frozen so
that $\Gamma_g$ is effectively constant and the locally coherent scalaron is
well described by the usual averaged quadratic-fluid closure, then with
\[
 \tau=\Gamma_gt,\qquad
 y=\frac{\rho_\phi}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}^2\Gamma_g^2},\qquad
 z=\frac{\rho_r}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}^2\Gamma_g^2},\qquad
 h=\sqrt{\frac{y+z}{3}},
\]
the homogenized system is parameter-free,
\[
 y'=-(3h+1)y,\qquad z'=-4hz+y.
\]
On the corresponding large-initial-$H/\Gamma_g$ comparison attractor, radiation equality obeys
\[
 \boxed{\frac{H_{\rm eq}}{\Gamma_g}=0.58009666626\ldots,}
 \qquad
 \boxed{\frac{\rho_{r,{\rm eq}}}
 {\bar M_{\rm Pl}^2\Gamma_g^2}=0.50476821331\ldots.}
\]
For a supplied physical \(\Gamma_g\) and thermally equilibrated relativistic
bath,
\[
 \boxed{T_{\rm eq}=
 \left[\frac{30}{\pi^2g_*}(0.50476821331\ldots)
 \bar M_{\rm Pl}^2\Gamma_g^2\right]^{1/4}.}
\]
These constants belong to the additional frozen-mass homogenized comparison.  They are not asserted as the exact global evolution of the restored running-mass branch.  A unique numerical temperature still requires the physical running couplings and kinetic/thermalization domain.
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[No independent K16 damping datum]
\label{p9:cor:v186-no-inserted-k16-damping}
The fundamental post-K16 scalaron equation contains the same-action Hubble
term $3H\dot F$ and the generated quantum source $Q_g$ when occupation is
present.  A replacement $3H\mapsto3H+\Gamma_{\rm phen}$ with an independently
supplied $\Gamma_{\rm phen}>0$ is not licensed.  Any effective decay rate is
an output such as Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:v186-running-mass-gamma} on its continuum,
state and WKB domain.
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[MIX16 factorization and literature kinetic interface]
\label{p9:thm:v181-mix16-factorization}
Separate thermal mixing into
\[
 \boxed{\mathrm{MIX16}=\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm kin}
 \oplus\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm KMS}.}
\]
The physically required reheating statement \(\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm kin}\) is
local kinetic/chemical equilibration of the underoccupied ordinary-sector
cascade on a timescale short compared with expansion and the chosen completion
surface.  On a supplied weakly coupled quasiparticle domain, the standard
gauge-plasma effective kinetic theory with leading \(2\leftrightarrow2\)
scattering and LPM-resummed effective \(1\leftrightarrow2\) splitting provides
the admitted microscopic interface
\cite{AMY2003EKT,KurkelaMoore2011Thermalization,HarigayaMukaida2014Reheating,DreesNajjari2022Cascade,MukaidaYamada2022SMCascade,MukaidaYamada2024YMReheating}.
Accordingly
\[
 \boxed{\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm kin}
 =\texttt{CLOSED\_DEPENDENT\_ON\_RG/KINETIC\_DOMAIN}}
\]
provided the K16 source/history, physical \(g_i(\mu)\), weak-coupling and
quasiparticle conditions, active scattering/splitting network, cosmological
injection domain, and any exact conserved charges are supplied.

The stronger statement \(\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm KMS}\) is exact limiting KMS
convergence of the full interacting many-body system.  It consumes the
additional Paper~IV reservoir/quasi-local analytic hypotheses and is therefore
\[
 \boxed{\mathrm{MIX16}_{\rm KMS}=\texttt{CLAIM\_SCOPED\_ANALYTIC\_LIMIT}.}
\]
It is not required to license the ordinary FLRW use of a local plasma
temperature once kinetic/chemical equilibration is established.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Native common-heat numbers are diagnostics, not physical plasma rates]
\label{p9:rem:v181-commonheat-rate-firewall}
Substituting \(g_i^{\rm heat}\) directly into the preceding formulas reproduces
the native-boundary diagnostic
\(A_g=0.0371314809117606\ldots\),
\(\Gamma_g\simeq1.15035\,{\rm GeV}\), and, for \(g_*=106.75\),
\(T_{\rm eq}\simeq5.79\times10^8\,{\rm GeV}\).  These numbers are typed
\texttt{COMMON\_HEAT\_NATIVE\_BOUNDARY\_DIAGNOSTIC}: the boundary value
\(\alpha_s^{\rm heat}=\pi/2\) is not weakly coupled, and the physical K16 hard
and plasma couplings require the gauge-sector $\mathrm{RG16}^{g}_{\rm match/run}$ map.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Post-seed scalar-to-Higgs pair vertex]
\label{p9:cor:postseed-higgs-pair-vertex}
Choose the declared RG/thermal matching point on the \(C_2\) background and
use \(\phi_c\) as the local scalar displacement there.  Let
\(e_0\in\Lambda^0_{\mathbb C}\subset\mathcal E_9\) denote its scalar source
direction.  On the Higgs quadratic-form domain, the corresponding block of
the third-variation vertex satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:postseed-higgs-pair-vertex}
 \boxed{
 \bigl\langle h,V_{16}^{H,T}(e_0)h\bigr\rangle
 =g_1(T,\alpha)\,\|h\|^2,
 \qquad
 g_1(T,\alpha)
 =-\frac{2\alpha^{-1/2}A_t(T)y_t\beta_y}{\bar M_{\rm Pl}}.}
\end{equation}
Consequently this scalar block is nonzero whenever
\(T>0\) and \(y_t\beta_y\ne0\).  Thus the existing Paper--VI-B action supplies the post-seed
scalar-to-Higgs feedback block once a nonzero thermal state exists.  It does
not prove nonvanishing on the spinor/vector summands of \(\mathcal E_9\), and
because \(A_t(0)=0\) it is not the first-bath source.  The first ordinary
zero-temperature seed is instead the gauge-pair block of
Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:v180-k16-zeroT-gauge-seed}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Polarize the \(H^\dagger H\) term in
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:closed-portal}.  Differentiation once in the scalar source
direction and twice in the Higgs direction gives the displayed Hermitian
quadratic form.  Since \(A_t=N_cT^2/12\), its vanishing and nonvanishing
conditions are exactly those stated.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact \(\alpha\)--\(\delta\) portal factors]
\label{p9:cor:alpha-delta-portal-factors}
Write
\[
 \alpha_{\rm top}:=e^{4\pi^2},
 \qquad \alpha=e^{4\pi^2+\delta}=\alpha_{\rm top}e^\delta.
\]
Holding the RG and thermal data fixed while comparing only this geometric
normalization, and assuming the reference coefficients
\(g_1(0)\ne0\) and \(g_2(0)\ne0\) so their ratios exist, the linear and
quadratic portal coefficients obey
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:alpha-delta-portal-factors}
 \boxed{
 \frac{g_1(\delta)}{g_1(0)}=e^{-\delta/2},
 \qquad
 \frac{g_2(\delta)}{g_2(0)}=e^{-\delta},}
 \qquad
 g_2=
 \frac{\alpha^{-1}A_t(\beta_y^2+y_tD\beta_y)}
 {\bar M_{\rm Pl}^{2}}.
\end{equation}
Hence \(e^{4\pi^2}\) and \(e^{4\pi^2+\delta}\) are not competing collision
weights.  The smooth completion multiplies the same-action pair kernel by
the displayed factors.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Use \(\alpha^{-1/2}=e^{-2\pi^2}e^{-\delta/2}\) and
\(\alpha^{-1}=e^{-4\pi^2}e^{-\delta}\) in
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:closed-portal}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Same-action Higgs mode-jump and sudden-squeeze bridge]
\label{p9:thm:higgs-mode-jump-squeeze-bridge}
On the post-seed Higgs branch, suppose the complete positive ordered kernel
has a common endpoint mode basis and, to the displayed RG/Weyl order, mode
eigenvalues
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:higgs-mode-kernel-endpoints}
 \Omega_{\lambda,\pm}^{2}
 =\Omega_{\lambda,0}^{2}
  +g_1(T,\alpha)\phi_{c,\pm}
  +g_2(T,\alpha)\phi_{c,\pm}^{2}>0.
\end{equation}
Then the endpoint ratio fixes, rather than assumes,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:higgs-derived-mode-jump}
 \boxed{
 d_\lambda
 =\frac14\log\frac{\Omega_{\lambda,-}^{2}}
                         {\Omega_{\lambda,+}^{2}},
 \qquad
 |\beta_\lambda|^2=\sinh^2d_\lambda}
\end{equation}
in the zero-width commuting-mode limit of Paper~IV.  Thus the direct ansatz
\(d_\lambda=w_\lambda\delta\) is unnecessary on this branch: \(\delta\)
enters through the derived coefficients
\eqref{p9:eq:alpha-delta-portal-factors}, and the physical background
endpoints determine the actual jump.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Positivity defines \(\Omega_{\lambda,\pm}>0\).  Solving
\(\Omega_{\lambda,+}/\Omega_{\lambda,-}=e^{-2d_\lambda}\) gives the first
formula.  Paper~IV's profile-independent sudden-layer theorem then gives the
second.  No collision kernel or equilibrium state enters this coherent
Bogoliubov calculation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Final-octet \(K=48\) onset and native-carrier desert]
\label{p9:thm:closed-j48-scalaron-abundance-trace}
On the active grade-four \(n_4\mid n_7\) branch of Paper~VI-A, the
homogeneous dark-response onset is inherited from the independently derived
\emph{final-octet separator}, not from reflection of the active dressing grade:
\[
 K_{\rm sh}^{\rm onset}=K_{\rm term}-8=56-8=48,
 \qquad j_{\rm cDM}:=K_{\rm sh}^{\rm onset}/8=6.
\]
Thus the same geometric hierarchy labels give
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:qdm6-kdm48-map}
\boxed{
j_{\rm cDM}=6,\qquad K_{\rm cDM}=48,\qquad
C_6=U\alpha^{-6}=\alpha C_7.}
\end{equation}
The integer \(6\) here labels the \emph{separator/onset}, not the selected
ultraviolet dressing depth \(j_{\rm dress}=4\). The prior identity
\(J(2)=8-2=6\) remains a valid arithmetic identity \emph{only in the
historical grade-two comparison}; it does not prove the current dark-response
lineage, because \(J(4)=4\). The actual geometric source is Paper~VI-A's
primitive-remnant depletion and canonical H/Hopf current on \(48\le K\le56\).
At onset, in the native carrier calculus,
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:k48-native-carrier-zero}
\boxed{P_{48}^{\rm act}
 =\mathbf1_{\{6\}}(\mathcal Q_{\rm act})=0.}
\end{equation}
For comparison only, the separate historical \(n_2\mid n_7\)
source-free local jet obeys the Paper~III-E result
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:k48-no-action-junction}
 \boxed{(\mathfrak f_{2\mid7}^{\rm act})''(C_6)
 =\chi_7^{\rm jet}(C_6)>0,
 \qquad C_6\notin
 \operatorname{sing\,supp}d\!\left((\widehat{\mathfrak f}_{2\mid7})'\right).}
\end{equation}
This historical jet formula is \emph{not} an action-owner assertion about
the current \(n_4\mid n_7\) branch. The final-octet geometric response
supplies neither a native \(n_6\) term nor a grade-six particle species,
rest-energy, occupation number, or physical stress tensor. Paper~IV's
absent-target theorem gives zero \emph{native quadratic production into the
absent carrier}; physical H-modality source/stress and cosmic-history
realization retain their separate occurrence conditions.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Paper~VI-A, Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:final-octet-primitive-remnant-depletion}
identifies \(Q_D=q_{\rm sp}-6=(K-48)/8\) on the final octet and shows that
its initial trace unit is not a rank-one particle. Its canonical Hopf and
homogeneous Hodge projections are Theorems~\ref{p8:thm:final-octet-canonical-hopf-descent}
and \ref{p8:thm:final-octet-flrw-hodge-dilution}. Together they locate the
onset at \(K=48\), independently of any reflected dressing rank.
The hierarchy's \(K=8j\), \(C_j=U\alpha^{-j}\) gives the displayed
coordinate identities. Paper~III-C's active carrier spectrum excludes grade
six, and Corollary~\ref{p4:cor:absent-target-production} excludes production
into that absent one-particle target. The old grade-two local Hessian and
absence of a grade-two-branch atom are \emph{only} the assertion of
Paper~III-E, Corollary~\ref{p3e:cor:unique-k16-interior-action-junction};
they are not used to establish the current grade-four source or its physical
H-modality occupation. No new dark-matter particle follows from the integer
48.\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Final-octet onset versus native mass gap]
\label{p9:cor:v165-reflected-onset-native-mass-gap}
The same numerical label \(K=48\) has two noninterchangeable typed uses.
On the native carrier it is the empty logarithmic centre
\[
 E_{48}=\sqrt{E_{40}E_{56}},
 \qquad
 \mathbf1_{(E_{56},E_{40})}(H_{\rm car})=0,
\]
which proves the finite carrier mass gap.  On the cosmological clock it is
the zero of \(Q_D=(K-48)/8\) and hence the onset label for a separately
derived final-octet response.  The latter does not supply the carrier absent
in the former.  Neither statement is a continuous Yang--Mills mass-gap
theorem.
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Final-octet common neutral parent of cosmic response and RAR]
\label{p9:thm:final-octet-dual-dark-response-parent}
The Paper--VI-A final-octet theorems import the exact identities
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:imported-final-octet-common-parent}
 \boxed{
 Q_D=q_{\rm sp}-6=\frac{K-48}{8},
 \qquad
 J_D=Q_D\Omega_H,
 \qquad
 \Omega_H=\frac{\eta_0\wedge d\eta_0}{4\pi^2}.}
\end{equation}
They have two canonical geometric projections:
\begin{align}
 \label{p9:eq:imported-final-octet-hodge-projection}
 \boxed{(\star_4J_D)^{\sharp\mu}}
 &=\boxed{\widehat\zeta_Du^\mu},
 &\widehat\zeta_D&=\frac{Q_D}{2\pi^2a^3},\\
 \label{p9:eq:imported-final-octet-hopf-projection}
 \boxed{\pi_!J_D}
 &=\boxed{Q_D\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}}.
\end{align}
Hence
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:imported-terminal-dual-parent}
 \boxed{
 K=56:\qquad
 a^3\widehat\zeta_D=\frac1{2\pi^2},
 \qquad
 \pi_!J_D=\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}.}
\end{equation}
The first projection is the neutral geometric parent of the homogeneous
cosmic-response current; the second is exactly the invariant measure used by
the static RAR readout.  Thus cDM and RAR are not independent geometric roots,
but neither are they the same physical stress tensor: their modalities remain
homogeneous \(H\) and static/terminal \(\{S,T\}\), respectively.  Physical
same-action occurrence, the dimensionful Hilbert-stress conversion, and the
static ownership premises remain separately typed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is the direct import of Paper~VI-A
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:final-octet-primitive-remnant-depletion} and
Theorems~\ref{p8:thm:final-octet-canonical-hopf-descent} and
\ref{p8:thm:final-octet-flrw-hodge-dilution}.  The endpoint values follow from
\(Q_D(56)=1\).  The distinct physical modalities are
Corollary~\ref{p8:cor:scalaron-five-role-support}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Optional reheating scalaron-excitation stress--energy]
\label{p9:def:scalaron-stress-energy-split}
Let \(\{\lambda\}\) be the finite scalaron momentum-mode set supplied by the
Paper--IV oscillator/transfer interface, with physical momentum
\(p_\lambda(a)\), occupation \(n_\lambda(a)\), and
\(E_\lambda(a)=\sqrt{m_\varphi^2c^4+p_\lambda(a)^2c^2}\).  On physical volume
\(V(a)\), define
\begin{align}
\rho_{\rm kin}(a)c^2
&:=\frac1{V(a)}\sum_\lambda E_\lambda(a)n_\lambda(a),\\
p_{\rm kin}(a)
&:=\frac1{3V(a)}\sum_\lambda
 \frac{p_\lambda(a)^2c^2}{E_\lambda(a)}n_\lambda(a).
\label{p9:eq:scalaron-kinetic-stress}
\end{align}
For a homogeneous canonical coherent mode \(\phi_c(t)\), define
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:scalaron-coherent-stress}
\rho_{\rm coh}c^2=\frac12\dot\phi_c^2+V_E(\phi_c),
\qquad
p_{\rm coh}=\frac12\dot\phi_c^2-V_E(\phi_c).
\end{equation}
The total scalaron-excitation stress--energy is the sum of these two
components.  This ledger is available for reheating/BBN propagation and is
not a dark-matter component of the framework.  These optional scalaron modes
are source-side excitations; they are not the ordered one-particle output
support \(\mathcal H_{\rm out}^{\rm ord}\) of
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:vertex-source-and-occupation-support}.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Physical scalaron histories are not structural resolution trajectories]
\label{p9:rem:v148-physical-scalaron-history-firewall}
All scalaron stresses in Definition~\ref{p9:def:scalaron-stress-energy-split}
are evaluated on an actual physical-time solution.  Paper~VI-A v2.51 proves
that the structural owner trajectory \(\varrho_{\rm str}(\tau)\) and the
physical scalaron trajectory \(R(t)\) share hierarchy landmarks but are not
generically the same path or parameterization.  In particular, on the exact
source-free lower active branch the scalaron has a damped oscillator energy
with natural equilibrium \(R_0>C_7\); the structural limit \(q_{\rm sp}\to7\)
therefore does not prove that a physical solution reaches \(R=C_7\).
Consequently no Paper--VI-B reheating, BBN, curvature-fluid, or terminal readout
may substitute \(\varrho_{\rm str}(\tau)\) for \(R(t)\) or infer a terminal
physical history solely from the structural endpoint.  Actual terminal arrival
and the scalaron-to-Einstein handoff retain their Paper--VI-A occurrence and
matching hypotheses.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Transported hot/cold kinematic split]
\label{p9:thm:transported-hot-cold-kinematic-split}
Assume the physical pole-mass ancestry used by Paper~VI-A and let an ordered
mode produced at \(K=16\) have its rest mass on a later hierarchy rung
\(K_m\ge32\).  Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:transported-produced-radiation-bound}
 \boxed{
 \frac{m}{E(16)}\le\alpha^{-1/2}
 =2.5221998\times10^{-9},
 \qquad
 0\le\frac13-w_{\rm ord}
 \le\frac{\alpha^{-1}}3
 =2.1204973\times10^{-18}.}
\end{equation}
Thus this ordered output is radiation-like independently of whether a KMS
state has formed.

For the retained homogeneous lower-branch scalaron \(X\), suppose
\(\ddot X+3H\dot X+m_X^2X=0\), \(m_X^2>0\), and the branch is underdamped with a slowly varying expansion history,
\(|H|/m_X\ll1\) and \(|\dot H|/m_X^2\ll1\), on a bounded comparison
interval. Averaging over oscillations gives the leading adiabatic behaviour
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:transported-retained-scalaron-matter-bound}
 \boxed{\langle w_X\rangle\simeq0,
 \qquad \langle\rho_X\rangle\propto a^{-3}
 \quad\text{(leading adiabatic order)}.}
\end{equation}
This is a matter-like source-side kinematic law, not a particle-DM
identification and not an abundance theorem.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Paper~VI-A's later-rung estimate gives \(m/E(16)\le\alpha^{-1/2}\).
For an isotropic relativistic ensemble,
\(w=\langle p^2/(3E^2)\rangle\), so
\(0\le1/3-w\le m^2/(3E^2)\), which gives the first box.  On the adiabatic
massive oscillator branch, the averaged virial relation
\(\langle\dot X^2\rangle\simeq m_X^2\langle X^2\rangle\) gives
small averaged pressure, and the continuity equation then gives
leading-order \(a^{-3}\) dilution; no exact global identity at finite
\(H/m_X\) is asserted.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Scope of a particle-yield conversion]
\label{p9:thm:scalaron-cold-yield-criterion}
Let \(Y_\varphi=n_\varphi/s\) be a conserved post-production number yield.
The familiar conversion
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:cold-yield-conversion}
\omega_{\varphi,\rm kin}
=\frac{s_0m_\varphi Y_\varphi}{\rho_{c,0}/h^2}
\end{equation}
would be valid only when the occupied modes are nonrelativistic at the evaluation
slice, in the sense that
\(p_\lambda(a_0)/(m_\varphi c)\ll1\) on the support carrying the yield.  In the
opposite relativistic limit, \(p_{\rm kin}\simeq\rho_{\rm kin}c^2/3\) and the
component belongs in the radiation ledger.  Since the framework does not use
scalaron particles as dark matter, Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:cold-yield-conversion} is
retained only as an exclusion/scope test for optional reheating excitations.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For each occupied mode,
\[
E_\lambda=m_\varphi c^2\sqrt{1+p_\lambda^2/(m_\varphi^2c^2)}.
\]
Uniformly small momentum ratios give
\(E_\lambda=m_\varphi c^2[1+O(p_\lambda^2/m_\varphi^2c^2)]\), so
\(\rho_{\rm kin}=m_\varphi n_\varphi[1+o(1)]\) and
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:cold-yield-conversion} follows from entropy conservation.  If
the ratios are uniformly large, \(E_\lambda\simeq p_\lambda c\) and the second
formula in Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:scalaron-kinetic-stress} gives
\(p_{\rm kin}\simeq\rho_{\rm kin}c^2/3\).  Number yield alone therefore does
not determine the cosmological equation of state.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Thermal scalaron excitations do not generate cosmic DM]
\label{p9:prop:thermal-freezein-not-cold}
Assume, as an explicit standard-thermal comparison branch, that a produced
scalaron mode at reheating has physical momentum
\(p_{\rm reh}c\ge \xi k_BT_{\rm reh}\) for some fixed \(\xi>0\), and that it
subsequently free-streams with conserved comoving momentum and standard entropy
redshift.  Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:thermal-momentum-today}
p_0c\ge \xi k_BT_{\gamma,0}
\left(\frac{g_{*S,0}}{g_{*S,\rm reh}}\right)^{1/3}.
\end{equation}
With \(T_{\gamma,0}=2.7255\,\mathrm K\),
\(g_{*S,0}=3.909\), and \(g_{*S,\rm reh}=427/4\), the lower bound is
\(p_0c\simeq7.8\times10^{-5}\xi\,\mathrm{eV}\).  This is a reheating
radiation/kinematics statement.  It is not evaluated against a K48 particle
mass, because no such dark-matter mass exists in the framework.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Entropy conservation gives
\(aTg_{*S}^{1/3}=\mathrm{const}\), while collisionless momentum redshifts as
\(p\propto a^{-1}\).  Eliminating the scale-factor ratio yields
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:thermal-momentum-today}.  Substitution of the displayed
thermal values gives the stated numerical lower bound
\(p_0c\simeq7.8\times10^{-5}\xi\,\mathrm{eV}\).  The conclusion is
conditional on the explicitly stated thermal-momentum branch; it does not
assume such a branch for an arbitrary finite transfer state.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Exact total elasticity and all positive homogeneous partitions]
\label{p9:cor:closed-abundance-scaling}
Let \(\mathfrak F,\mathfrak M>0\) be \(C^1\) on a connected open
positive-parameter domain \(\mathcal U\subset(0,\infty)^d\), and set
\(\mathfrak A=\mathfrak F+\mathfrak M\).  For each varied parameter
\(x_i\), the exact additive elasticity is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:additive-elasticity}
\frac{\partial\log\mathfrak A}{\partial\log x_i}
=\frac{\mathfrak F}{\mathfrak A}
 \frac{\partial\log\mathfrak F}{\partial\log x_i}
+\frac{\mathfrak M}{\mathfrak A}
 \frac{\partial\log\mathfrak M}{\partial\log x_i}.
\end{equation}
For prescribed constants \(\kappa_i\), the total has the exact global
product law \(\mathfrak A=C\prod_{i=1}^d x_i^{\kappa_i}\) on
\(\mathcal U\) if and only if the \emph{weighted sum} above equals
\(\kappa_i\) for every \(i\) and every point of \(\mathcal U\).
Equality of component elasticities is sufficient but \emph{not necessary}.
Indeed, all positive splits of any such product-law total are precisely
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v1201-homogeneous-positive-partitions}
 \boxed{\mathfrak F=\varphi\mathfrak A,\quad
 \mathfrak M=(1-\varphi)\mathfrak A,\quad
 0<\varphi(x)<1,}
\end{equation}
where \(\varphi\) may be any \(C^1\) function on \(\mathcal U\).
Thus the split energy fractions alone do not establish a total scaling law;
they must be propagated as functions of the actual sourced state/history.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Differentiate \(\mathfrak A=\mathfrak F+\mathfrak M\) and divide by
\(\mathfrak A\).  If each total elasticity is a constant \(\kappa_i\),
all coordinate derivatives of
\(\log\mathfrak A-\sum_i\kappa_i\log x_i\) vanish on connected
\(\mathcal U\), making it the constant \(\log C\).  The converse follows
by differentiation.  Writing \(\varphi=\mathfrak F/\mathfrak A\)
parametrizes all positive splits, with individual elasticities
\[
 \frac{\partial\log\mathfrak F}{\partial\log x_i}
 =\kappa_i+\frac{\partial\log\varphi}{\partial\log x_i},\quad
 \frac{\partial\log\mathfrak M}{\partial\log x_i}
 =\kappa_i+\frac{\partial\log(1-\varphi)}{\partial\log x_i}.
\]
The variable split \(\mathfrak F=x^2/(1+x)\) and
\(\mathfrak M=x/(1+x)\) on \(x>0\) has exact total
\(\mathfrak A=x\) but unequal component elasticities, directly excluding
the earlier claimed necessity.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[First-split energy ledger, not universal abundance multiplier]
\label{p9:thm:scalaron-readout-coordinate-conversion}
On the optional Paper--VI-A \(\mathrm{DEM}_{15}+\mathrm{Hkin}\) first-split
branch, the already typed source-energy ledger gives
\[
B_{\rm vis}=\frac35,\qquad B_{\rm coh}=\frac25,
\qquad T_{\rm reh}^{\rm HB}=B_{\rm vis}^{1/4}T_{\rm reh}^{(0)}.
\]
These statements fix an energy split and a visible-bath temperature.  They do
not imply a universal multiplier \(B_{\rm vis}^{-3/8}\) for the total scalaron
abundance.  Kinetic and coherent components must instead be propagated
separately and then added.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{p9:cor:closed-abundance-scaling} gives the exact necessary
and sufficient \emph{weighted-total} elasticity condition for a product law.
The finite \(3/5\)--\(2/5\) split fixes one source-energy ledger, but does
not determine the scale dependence of either kinetic or coherent contribution
or their partition function.  It therefore cannot determine a universal
total-abundance multiplier, even though unequal component elasticities would
not by themselves exclude a product-law total.
\end{proof}




\paragraph{Reheating-state closure boundary.}
The rigorous output is a scalaron-excitation stress--energy functional for the
separate reheating/BBN interface, not a dark-matter relic number.  Once a finite
production map supplies \(n_\lambda(a)\) and a coherent initial state supplies
\((\phi_c,\dot\phi_c)\), Eqs.~\eqref{p9:eq:scalaron-kinetic-stress}--
\eqref{p9:eq:scalaron-coherent-stress} determine the scalaron density and
pressure without a new phenomenological normalization.

% Source Paper VI-B, original line 1785
\subsubsection{Cosmic effective-curvature and RAR interfaces}
\label{p9:sec:scalaron-phenomenology-interfaces}
Let \(f=f_{2\mid7}^{\rm act}\), \(F=df/d\scal\), and
\(\kappa=8\pi G/c^4\).  Rearranging the metric-\(f(\scal)\) equations gives
the exact Einstein-form identity
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:exact-curvature-effective-tensor}
G_{\mu\nu}=\frac{\kappa}{F}T_{\mu\nu}
+\frac1F\left[
 \frac12(f-F\scal)g_{\mu\nu}
 +\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu F-g_{\mu\nu}\Box F
\right].
\end{equation}
The second term is geometric.  It is therefore legitimate to define its
FLRW energy-density and pressure readouts, in \(c=1\) notation, by
\begin{align}
\label{p9:eq:exact-curvature-fluid-readout}
\rho_{\rm curv}
 &=\frac{(F\scal-f)/2-3H\dot F}{\kappa F},\\
p_{\rm curv}
 &=\frac{\ddot F+2H\dot F+(f-F\scal)/2}{\kappa F}.
\end{align}
Equations~\eqref{p9:eq:exact-curvature-effective-tensor}--
\eqref{p9:eq:exact-curvature-fluid-readout} are algebraic consequences of
the same action; they do not add a matter field or a particle species.

\begin{theorem}[Optional exact material-current dust response from the final-octet neutral parent]
\label{p9:thm:k48-effective-dust-propagation}
This theorem is a stronger comparison branch and is not part of the native cDM
owner derivation.  Assume additionally that the occurring same-action geometry
realizes the imported neutral
Hodge current through one fixed physical owner coefficient \(\lambda_D\geq0\):
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:k48-physical-current-owner-map}
 \mathcal J_D^\mu
 =\lambda_D\widehat{\mathcal J}_D^\mu
 =\zeta u^\mu,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\zeta=\lambda_D\frac{Q_D}{2\pi^2a^3}}.
\end{equation}
If its local derivative-free isotropic energy density \(E(\zeta)\) is
continuous, \(E(0)=0\), and additive on \(\mathbb R_{\ge0}\), then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:k48-effective-dust-law}
\boxed{
E(\zeta)=\mu\zeta,
\qquad
p_{\rm cDM}=\zeta E'(\zeta)-E(\zeta)=0,
\qquad c_{s,{\rm cDM}}^2=0\quad(\mu>0).}
\end{equation}
During the final-octet transfer,
\begin{equation}
 \partial_t(a^3\zeta)=\frac{\lambda_D}{2\pi^2}\dot Q_D,
\end{equation}
and after terminal completion
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:k48-terminal-physical-dust-law}
 \boxed{
 \zeta=\frac{\lambda_D}{2\pi^2a^3},
 \qquad
 E(\zeta)=\frac{\mu\lambda_D}{2\pi^2a^3}.}
\end{equation}
The neutral-current construction entering this theorem is precisely the already
closed package \(\mathrm{K48GEOM}\).  Paper~VI-A v2.107 additionally closes
the finite carrier-energy law \(\mathrm{K48ENERGY}_{\rm finite}\) and the
native physical Hilbert owner through the co-realized homogeneous $H$ modality.
The theorem above therefore remains only a broad optional material-current
family parameterized by \(\lambda_D,\mu\); those parameters are not native K48
predictions and are not used to normalize the curvature-fluid cDM response.
The neutral comoving factor \(1/(2\pi^2)\) and the \(a^{-3}\) current shape
remain geometric imports from Paper~VI-A.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:final-octet-dual-dark-response-parent} gives the neutral
Hodge current and its balance.  Multiplication by constant \(\lambda_D\)
gives the physical current formula.  Continuity and additivity solve the
Cauchy equation as \(E(\zeta)=\mu\zeta\), and the isotropic derivative-free
variation gives \(p=\zeta E'-E=0\). If \(\mu>0\), \(dE/d\zeta=\mu>0\),
so \(dp/dE=0\). If \(\mu=0\), this optional branch carries no energy and
the ratio \(dp/dE\) is undefined rather than a measured zero sound speed.  At \(K=56\),
\(Q_D=1\), giving the terminal formulas.  No particle number current is
introduced.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Native finite-energy K48 candidate]
\label{p9:cor:v179-native-k48-finite-energy-candidate}
For the native final-octet branch, Paper~VI-A v2.106 gives
\[
 \varepsilon_{\rm car}
 =\frac{\sqrt{18/\pi}\,\hbar c_{\rm op}}{7R_{\rm mom}}
 \left(4+\alpha_{\rm hier}^{-1}
 +2\alpha_{\rm hier}^{-5/4}\right)>0
\]
and therefore the finite carrier-energy current has homogeneous density
\[
 \boxed{\rho_{D,\rm car}(a)
 =\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm car}Q_D}{2\pi^2a^3}.}
\]
This removes an arbitrary finite K48 exchange-energy coefficient.  The finite
candidate is a distinct bookkeeping descendant and is not identified with the
physical cosmological stress.  The native physical owner is instead the
co-realized homogeneous $H$-modality curvature response of Paper~VI-A
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v292-k48-h-modality-corealization}.
\end{corollary}

The other physical dark-response projection is the static RAR packet imported
from Paper~VI-A,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:rar-geometric-interface}
g_{\rm obs}=\nu\!\left(\frac{g_b}{a_0}\right)g_b,
\qquad
a_0=\frac{c^2}{4\pi}\sqrt{C_7}
=\frac{c^2}{4\pi\sqrt\alpha}\sqrt{C_6}.
\end{equation}
Its measure is not an independent geometric normalization:
\begin{equation}
 \label{p9:eq:rar-terminal-parent-measure}
 \boxed{d\mu_H=\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}
 =\left.\pi_!J_D\right|_{K=56}}.
\end{equation}
Thus cosmological growth and lensing propagate the homogeneous same-parent
projection in Eqs.~\eqref{p9:eq:exact-curvature-fluid-readout} and
\eqref{p9:eq:k48-effective-dust-law}, while galaxy tests propagate the
static same-parent projection in Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:rar-geometric-interface}.  No de Broglie wavelength,
ultralight-particle mass, occupation number, or halo-particle ansatz belongs
to either channel.

\subsubsection{Imported trace-response and viability boundary}
\label{p9:sec:imported-trace-viability-boundary}
Paper~VI-A owns the quadratic-dressed Laurent metric-\(f(\scal)\) trace response
on its open active branches, the sufficient
thin-shell/PPN recovery criterion, the scalar-frozen compact-object exterior,
and the galaxy-wide RAR kernel.  Paper~VI-B imports the separation
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:local-trace-mass-clean}
 m_R^2(\varrho)
 =\frac{F_{2\mid7}(\varrho)-\varrho\chi_{2\mid7}(\varrho)}
 {3\chi_{2\mid7}(\varrho)},
 \qquad
 \varrho\in\mathscr V_{2\mid7},
\end{equation}
where $m_R$ is the local Yukawa trace-response mass.  It is distinct from both
the homogeneous effective curvature fluid and the static RAR kernel; no
single fixed scalaron mass is substituted for either response.  As
\(\varrho\downarrow C_{56}\) the
active-side Laurent mass remains finite; after the
endpoint handoff the terminal quotient has no scalaron Hessian and its formal
algebraic scalaron mass is infinite.  Both endpoint atoms in the
distributional Hessian have positive derived weights; nonnegative
mollification therefore supplies convex smooth representatives.
The exact K16 interface carries no independent microscopic profile or width;
an actual crossing is history content subject to the upstream junction
condition.  Paper~VI-B does not reinterpret the endpoint as a finite terminal
pole mass or as a second Einstein--Hilbert term.

Solar-System covariance maps and strong-field evolution are not cosmological
readouts.  They remain tests or propagation problems of the Paper~VI-A gravity
action and are not duplicated here.

\subsection{Finite representation statements and physical-role boundaries}
\label{p9:sec:h0}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$D_4$ octet decomposition and density-role no-go}{D4 octet decomposition and density-role no-go}]
\label{p9:thm:d4-pure-density-projector}
For the triality-adapted Hurwitz sign cube and a chosen body diagonal,
\[
\mathcal N_8^+
=\{v_{+++},v_{---}\}\sqcup T_+\sqcup T_-,
\qquad |T_+|=|T_-|=3,
\]
so the corresponding representation space has the exact decomposition
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:d4-density-octet-split}
V_8=V_\parallel\oplus V_\perp,
\qquad \dim V_\parallel=2,
\qquad \dim V_\perp=6.
\end{equation}
The finite geometry alone does not select either summand as cosmological matter
or vacuum.  In particular, \(\Omega_m=1/4\) and \(\Omega_\Lambda=3/4\) are not
native consequences of Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:d4-density-octet-split}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The sign-cube/trimer decomposition proves the dimensions.  A cosmological
matter/vacuum label is extra semantic data: assigning
\((P_m,P_\Lambda)=(P_\parallel,P_\perp)\) and exchanging the two roles both
preserve the same finite decomposition and introduce no mathematical
contradiction.  Therefore the representation data do not distinguish the two
physical assignments.  The former density-role claim is removed rather than
replaced by a new owner selector.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Paper--V-B comparison overlap is representation dependent]
\label{p9:lem:typed-determinant-readout-functor}
The Paper--V-B comparison construction yields \(24/25\) on its orthogonal-label
carrier and \(4/5\) on its normalized vertex-incidence carrier.  Hence no
representation-independent scalar \(\eta\) is available for automatic
promotion to an optical, Hubble, BAO, damping, or density homothety.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Both values are exact Paper--V-B finite readouts on their declared carriers.
Since they differ while the upstream finite object is otherwise unchanged, a
physical optical action cannot be inferred from the number \(24/25\) alone.
The former \(\mathrm{OWN}_{25\to\rm opt}\) premise is eliminated from the
native Paper--VI-B chain by not making that promotion.
\end{proof}

% Source Paper VI-B, original line 1997
\subsection{Growth and active-resolution boundary}
\label{p9:sec:growth-boundary-repaired}
The current Paper--III source supplies rank-conditioned alternatives, not a
uniform physical probability measure over the eight ranks; it also supplies no
simultaneous \(1/k^2\) scalar Green spectrum.  Therefore the former determinant
built from \(H_8\) and \(\sum_{k=1}^8k^{-2}\), and the associated native
\(\sigma_8\) number, are removed.

\begin{lemma}[Canonical closed-slice scalar Green spectrum]
\label{p9:lem:s3-green-spectrum}
On a closed FLRW slice \(S^3(a)\), Paper~VI-A imports the scalar-harmonic
spectrum
\[
\Delta_{S^3(a)}Y_n=\frac{n(n+2)}{a^2}Y_n,
\qquad n=0,1,2,\ldots .
\]
On the mean-zero scalar subspace (the orthogonal complement of \(n=0\)), the
inverse Laplacian is therefore uniquely
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:s3-green-spectrum}
\Delta_{S^3(a)}^{-1}Y_n
=\frac{a^2}{n(n+2)}Y_n,
\qquad n\ge1.
\end{equation}
Thus the geometry does supply a canonical scalar spatial Green spectrum, but it
is \(a^2/[n(n+2)]\), not the previously asserted simultaneous \(1/k^2\)
rank spectrum.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The constant scalar harmonic is exactly the kernel of the Laplacian.  Every
positive-eigenvalue harmonic is invertible, and spectral functional calculus
gives the reciprocal eigenvalue.  This construction requires no probability
measure over the independent Paper--III rank alternatives.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Growth normalization boundary]
\label{p9:thm:growth-normalization-boundary}
Let \(\mathcal L_{\rm pert}[F_{2\mid7},\chi_{2\mid7},H]\) denote the linear
scalar-perturbation operator obtained by perturbing the active Paper--VI-A
metric-\(f(\scal)\) action on an open domain contained in
\(\mathscr V_{2\mid7}\).  Retain the initial perturbation data and the physical
matter transfer/window with the background history.  On the native free
cyclic quadratic branch the birth covariance and background are generated;
generic preparations retain their supplied data.  The actual material-source
transfer and continuation must be licensed on the late claim domain.  Then
the late growth field is the solution of
\[
\mathcal L_{\rm pert}[F_{2\mid7},\chi_{2\mid7},H]D=0
\]
with those retained initial data.  The absolute statistic \(\sigma_8\) also
consumes the actual material-source transfer, evolution and 8\(h^{-1}\)Mpc
window.  Native birth normalization is reused on its quadratic domain; it is
not independently fitted or recreated at this consumer.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Linearization of a fixed action fixes the linear differential operator.
Solutions of a homogeneous linear perturbation equation consume their initial
data; a licensed native covariance supplies those moments without another
preparation.  The statistic \(\sigma_8\) is a quadratic windowed normalization
of the actual resulting matter power spectrum.  Neither a probability measure over the
Paper--III rank alternatives nor a \(1/k^2\) Green spectrum is generated by
the current upstream corpus, so no additional determinant normalization is
available.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Historical synchronization convention; not a native hierarchy theorem]
\label{p9:prop:conditional-protected-log-frequency}
The earlier optional convention
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:conditional-log-frequency}
\Delta\ln k=\frac{\ln\alpha}{7},
\qquad
\omega=\frac{14\pi}{\ln\alpha}
\end{equation}
is retained only for backwards comparison.  It is not inherited from the
current protected-depth character, which obeys \(q_{\rm sp}=j\Rightarrow
H_j^{\rm REG}=\alpha^j\).  A native horizon-crossing frequency requires a
generated cross-owner evolution relation between protected depth and the
causal variable \(\ln(a/R_A)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under the current depth character one unit of \(q_{\rm sp}\) already carries
one \(\ln\alpha\) determinant-depth increment; seven such units therefore do
not furnish one \(\ln\alpha\) step.  Since an arbitrary monotone
reparameterization between the internal depth and cosmological time preserves
each owner geometry while changing the synchronization, no native frequency
follows without an additional cross-owner flow theorem.
\end{proof}

For a solved zero-mean residual \(\mathcal S_{\rm sp}(k)\) and a normalized
survey window \(\mu_a(k)\), the exact downstream map remains
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:smooth-transfer-window-map}
\Delta\mathcal O_a=\int d\ln k\,\mu_a(k)\mathcal S_{\rm sp}(k).
\end{equation}
This is a validation pushforward, not a new selector.

% Source Paper VI-B, original line 2558
\subsection{Physical-density ledger and finite-SM handoffs}
\label{p9:sec:exact-density-closure}
Define \(h=H_0/H_{100}\) and \(\omega_i=\Omega_i h^2\).  On the late frozen
branch the present closure is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:density-closure}
h^2(1-\Omega_{k,0})
=\omega_\Lambda+\omega_b+\omega_{\rm cDM}
 +\omega_\nu+\omega_r+\omega_{\rm other\,matter}.
\end{equation}
Within the dust+vacuum reduction of
Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:late-density-ratio-repaired}, radiation is omitted from the
clock theorem itself; it can be restored in the exact present ledger by the
explicit \(\omega_r\) term above.

\begin{definition}[Imported finite-SM cosmology boundary]
\label{p9:def:imported-finite-sm-cosmology-boundary}
Paper~VI-B may consume the Paper--V-C baryon benchmark \(\eta_B^{\rm tr}\), the
thermal-history quantities \(N_{\rm eff}^{\rm th}\) and entropy transfer, and
the compatible Paper--V-B neutrino spectrum, but it inherits their conditional
statuses.  No cosmological comparison may upgrade them to upstream theorems.
\end{definition}

Conditional on a supplied \(\eta_B\),
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:omega-b-from-eta}
\omega_b=\frac{8\pi G}{3H_{100}^2}
 m_p\eta_B\frac{2\zeta(3)}{\pi^2}
 \left(\frac{k_BT_{\gamma,0}}{\hbar c}\right)^3.
\end{equation}
The Paper--V-C benchmark used in the current corpus gives
\(\omega_b\simeq0.02242\), but this numerical value remains conditional on
that baryogenesis branch.

On the standard neutrino thermal history and in the late nonrelativistic
regime,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:omega-nu-handoff}
\omega_\nu=\frac{\Sigma_\nu}{93.14\,\mathrm{eV}}.
\end{equation}
For the conditional Paper--V-B sum \(\Sigma_\nu\simeq59.95\,\mathrm{meV}\),
this is \(\omega_\nu\simeq6.4366\times10^{-4}\).

\begin{corollary}[Residual cosmic effective-curvature target]
\label{p9:cor:H0-dens-closed-baryon}
If, in addition to the late clock theorem, all ordinary-matter components are
supplied, then the background value required of the conditional cosmic
effective-curvature response is the residual
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:omega-phi-residual-repaired}
\boxed{\omega_{\rm cDM}
=\omega_m-\omega_b-\omega_\nu-\omega_{\rm other\,dust}.}

or normalization of the effective current.  It is not assigned to the

\subsection{Massive-neutrino transfer with correct multi-species weighting}
\label{p9:sec:massive-neutrino-handoff}
\begin{theorem}[Energy-density weighted neutrino transfer]
\label{p9:thm:massive-neutrino-transfer-growth-lensing}
For species transfer functions \(T_{\nu i}(k,z)\) and background energy
densities \(\rho_{\nu i}(z)\), the total neutrino density transfer is
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:total-neutrino-transfer}
T_\nu(k,z)
=\frac{\sum_i\rho_{\nu i}(z)T_{\nu i}(k,z)}
       {\sum_i\rho_{\nu i}(z)}.
\end{equation}
The total matter transfer is then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:total-matter-transfer-repaired}
T_m=(1-f_\nu)T_{cb}+f_\nu T_\nu,
\qquad f_\nu=\rho_\nu/\rho_m.
\end{equation}
Bare mass weighting is recovered only in the nonrelativistic equal-number-density
limit \(\rho_{\nu i}\propto m_{\nu i}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A density transfer is defined by \(\delta\rho_\nu=\sum_i\delta\rho_{\nu i}\).
Writing \(\delta\rho_{\nu i}=\rho_{\nu i}T_{\nu i}\mathcal R\) for a common
primordial source \(\mathcal R\) and factoring
\(\delta\rho_\nu=\rho_\nu T_\nu\mathcal R\) gives
Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:total-neutrino-transfer}.  The matter formula follows by the
same decomposition.  Replacing \(\rho_{\nu i}\) by \(m_i\) therefore requires
the stated late nonrelativistic thermal condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Boundary-only K48 homogeneous readout has no extra physical mode]
\label{p9:cor:v1107-k48-n0-readout}
On a regular source-constant segment with \(\dot a(s)\neq0\) and no
turning point between source and response, the exact raw homogeneous retarded
kernel is

 G_0^R(t,s)=\Theta(t-s)\frac{\dot a(t)}{\dot a(s)}.

 \boxed{(D_{48}H)_a=0.}

constituted source/integration constant; it is not generated by endpoint
boundary data alone.
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[Resolved cold scalar readout kernel]
\label{p9:cor:v1107-resolved-cold-readout}
For $n>2$ closed-$S^3$ scalar harmonics and an admitted regular expanding
segment with \(H(s)>0\), \(H(u)>0\) on the integration interval, and
stationary source/constraint linearization in the declared regime,
Paper~VI-A gives

 \mathcal G_{n,\rm con}^{R}(t,s)
 =\Theta(t-s)\frac{c^2}{3H(s)}
 \exp\!\left[-\int_s^t
 \left(H+\frac{c^2d_n}{3Ha^2}\right)du\right],

\]
The local/static limit is horizon suppressed.  This kernel is the cosmological
constraint/history response and is not to be double-counted as an additional
K48 force.
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[Frozen versus freely relaxed kinetic RAR susceptibility]
\label{p9:cor:v1107-kinetic-dynamic-readout}
On an admitted collisionless environmental branch, the causal source response is
\[
 \mathcal V_{\rm kin}^{R}
 =M_{\chi_{\rm kin}/F_\star}
 -\mathcal B\bar{\mathscr L}_R^{-1}\mathcal C.

\[
 \boxed{

 \boxed{
 \mathcal V_{\rm kin}^{R}(0)=\chi_{\rm rel}/F_\star.}
\]
At high frequency the source cannot rearrange and
$\mathcal V_{\rm kin}^{R}\to\chi_{\rm kin}/F_\star$.  Thus a static RAR
susceptibility is not by itself a unique dynamical matter law: the actual
source history/Casimirs determine which tangent is realized.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Remaining dynamic RAR readout frontier]
\label{p9:rem:v1107-moving-port-frontier}

\subsection{Boundary normalization and the remaining cold-density evaluation}
\label{p9:sec:v2203-boundary-readout}
The native cDM role remains the already co-realized homogeneous \(H\)
metric-response modality of
Theorem~\ref{p8:thm:v292-k48-h-modality-corealization}. The new identities
of Paper~III-F do not introduce another Hilbert owner or a second action.

source-normalized geometric excess, and an additional full-parent mean
are different allocations, as made explicit in
Proposition~\ref{p3f:prop:v2203-source-normalized-remainder} and
Corollary~\ref{p3f:cor:v2203-no-second-minimal-mean}.
Their source and vacuum pieces must be counted once before comparison.

\begin{corollary}[Exact RAR and terminal-boundary normalization bridge]
\label{p9:cor:v2203-rar-boundary-normalization}
Use \(c=1\), \(M^2=U/4\), \(\kappa=M^{-2}=8\pi G\), and the
existing static readout \(a_0=\sqrt{C_7}/(4\pi)\). On the separately
licensed asymptotic expanding affine-vacuum branch, the bare gravitational
endpoint scale of Theorem~\ref{p3f:thm:v2203-terminal-boundary-budget}
satisfies
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal B_\infty=4\pi\sqrt6\,M^2a_0,
 \qquad \rho_\Lambda=\frac{\mathcal B_\infty^2}{12M^2}
 =\frac{M^2C_7}{2}.}
\]
For an actually allocated candidate density \(\rho_D\), define
\(\nu_H=4\pi\kappa\rho_D/C_7\). Then
\[
 \rho_D=\frac{\nu_H}{2}\frac{a_0^2}{G},\qquad
 \frac{\rho_D}{\rho_\Lambda}=\frac{\nu_H}{2\pi}.
\]
The proposed \(\nu_H=2\) is therefore the target
\(\rho_D=a_0^2/G=\rho_\Lambda/\pi\), not a consequence of
\(Q_E=2\) or of the shared hierarchy coefficient.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The terminal theorem gives \(\mathcal B_\infty^2=6M^4C_7\).
Insert the existing \(a_0\) and \(G=1/(8\pi M^2)\); all displayed
identities follow. The topological Euler number in
Proposition~\ref{p1:prop:v2203-euler-spin-lift} contains no physical
energy per charge or observation-slice datum.
\end{proof}
The two readouts have different dimensions and physical roles. The
connection above is a normalization identity after consuming their actual
readout maps. In closed finite-radius affine vacuum,
\(\mathcal B^2=\mathcal B_\infty^2-36M^4k/a^2\); the asymptotic
scale is not a pointwise lower bound. The full matched
\(\Omega\) curvature momentum must not be substituted for this bare
affine momentum without its scalar/source endpoint contributions.

\begin{proposition}[Separate density and pressure tests on an observation slice]
\label{p9:prop:v2203-abundance-two-ports}
On the terminal metric domain let \(\rho_s,p_s\) be the already counted
comparison source and let \(\rho_D,p_D\) be a genuinely admitted
additional mean. With \(\mathcal B=6M^2H\) and \(K=k/a^2\),
\[
 \nu_H=\frac{2\pi}{\mathcal B_\infty^2}
 \left[\mathcal B^2-\mathcal B_\infty^2
       +36M^4K-12M^2\rho_s\right],
\]
while exact \(p_D=0\) independently requires
\[
 \dot{\mathcal B}=3\rho_\Lambda
 -\frac{\mathcal B^2}{4M^2}-3M^2K-3p_s.
\]
Consequently the proposed density on an independently identified finite
observation slice \(t_*\) requires both the displayed momentum budget
with \(\nu_H(t_*)=2\) and the actual pressure test there.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the source-subtracted density and pressure in
Theorem~\ref{p3f:thm:v2203-terminal-boundary-budget}, then the definition
of \(\nu_H\). These are the two physical work ports of
Proposition~\ref{p3f:prop:v2203-two-work-ports}; a single scalar
normalization cannot replace the independent spatial-scale variation.
\end{proof}
On the completed minimal gravity plus same finite source, this additional
mean is zero. A retained source identified as dark instead occupies its

subtraction. The finite-CAR and Gaussian cold-survival theorems,
\ref{p3f:thm:v2203-local-source-cold-tail} and
\ref{p4:thm:v2203-gaussian-cold-tail}, provide conditional source
mechanisms, not an automatic new carrier or numerical abundance.
At finite \(t_*\), evaluate \(E(t_*)=V_*\rho(t_*)\) and actual
\(p(t_*)\); replacing them by asymptotic \(E_\infty\) and zero
pressure consumes a controlled tail-error bound.

\begin{remark}[The exact remaining calculation]
\label{p9:rem:v2203-cold-density-frontier}
The geometry, action coefficient, metric variation and normalization

\subsection{Loaded boundary energy census and the positive cosmic target}
\label{p9:sec:v2206-loaded-energy-census}

Paper~VI-A, Section~\ref{p8:sec:v2206-static-baryonic-mean}, executes
the uniform lapse derivative of the specified relaxed static scalar
restriction. It distinguishes its positive stored field energy from
the complete negative induced correction, while preserving the
separate full Lorentzian $H/S/T$ problem.

\begin{proposition}[Shape weighting of the evaluated weak-source terms]
\label{p9:prop:v2206-loaded-shape-weighting}
On the small-source fixed-proper-size continuum diagnostic, let actual
body rest energies $m_i$ and radii $R_i$ be supplied, and use the leading
unit-loading self terms
$\Delta E_i=-c_iG_Nm_i^2/R_i$, with $c_i=3/5$ for a uniform sphere
and $c_i=8/(3\pi)$ for a uniform thin disk. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v2206-loaded-shape-weighting}
 \boxed{\beta_{\rm ind}
 =-\sum_i\frac{m_i}{M_b}c_i\frac{G_Nm_i}{R_i}
       +\frac{E_{\rm cross}}{M_b},\qquad M_b=\sum_i m_i.}
\end{equation}
This is a binding ratio, with $c=1$; in SI its compactness factor is
$G_Nm_i/(R_ic^2)$ for mass-valued $m_i$.
For the same positive drives the exact quadratic cross contribution is
\[
 E_{\rm cross}=-\frac{M^2}{2}
       \sum_{i<j}\eta_i\eta_j\langle J_i,GJ_j\rangle\le0,
\]
on the positivity-preserving static inverse. Thus local weak-field self
terms have magnitude controlled by their compactness, not by an average
of $\pi$ and $4\pi$. The local self terms alone cannot produce a positive
order-one cosmic abundance. A collective or nonlinear cosmic contribution
is a different complete-action calculation; it cannot be estimated by
extending this local perturbative result beyond its domain.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Divide the computed pair energies by the conserved rest-energy census.
Expand $\langle\sum\eta_iJ_i,G\sum\eta_jJ_j\rangle$ to obtain the cross
term. The maximum principle gives $\langle J_i,GJ_j\rangle\ge0$ for
the indicated nonnegative drives. Internal material equilibrium and
its kinetic/binding contribution remain in the source reference; the
scalar loading coefficient is not a full body mass calculation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The field-only assignment must retain its compensation]
\label{p9:prop:v2206-field-only-compensation}
On the executed quadratic static branch,
\[
 \beta_{\rm field}=-\beta_{\rm ind}\ge0,\qquad
 \beta_{\rm int}=-2\beta_{\rm field},\qquad
 \beta_{\rm field}+\beta_{\rm int}=\beta_{\rm ind}\le0.
\]
A census assigning the field term to a dark sector must keep its
interaction term in the remaining complete source/geometry allocation.
It then has the same total lapse variation. Counting the field term while
keeping the unloaded source unchanged changes that variation and cannot
establish an additional positive expansion density.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the field, interaction and stationary total terms of
Paper~III-F, Theorem~\ref{p3f:thm:v2206-loaded-static-energy}.
Reallocation preserves their sum, rather than generating another mean.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The next positive mean is an actual history output]
\label{p9:rem:v2206-positive-history-target}
If an independently admitted positive kinetic response block exists,
the completed square of Paper~III-F,
Remark~\ref{p3f:rem:v2206-positive-loaded-excitation}, distinguishes
its actual kinetic/displacement energy from the negative relaxed
loading term and the inherited baseline. The normalization target on
an identified observation slice is the full allocated energy
$E_\partial(t_*)=\beta_*M_b$, together with the independently tested
pressure and perturbation response. It is not the norm of the static
kernel or an unallocated covariance.

Its formation obeys the retained identity
\[
 E_\partial(t_*)-E_\partial(t_i)
 =\int_{t_i}^{t_*}V(Q_\partial-3Hp_\partial)dt.
\]
The actual state, coupling work, reference energy and clock must supply
that integral. The fixed terminal vacuum and a static relaxation alone
do not select the positive target. The source-free reference is not
reselected as an instantaneous ground during evolution. The background
ratio/pressure relation and the separate physical second-variation tests
of v2.205 continue to apply.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Retained excitation and the common cosmological response}
\label{p9:sec:v2209-retained-state-output}

\subsection{Validation-map boundary}
\label{p9:app:validation-map-boundary}

\begin{definition}[Validation map]
A validation map compares a retained predicted response with a declared
observer/data object.  A nuclear, radiation or nonlinear evolution generated
from the complete native action/source word is instead a response construction
or execution, even when absent from a reduced printed kernel.  If such an
operation is supplied conditionally rather than constructed, retain its input
and domain explicitly.  Catalog windows, noise and likelihood comparisons
retain their observational provenance.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[No-selector boundary]
\label{p9:thm:no-selector-boundary-clean}
Let $\mathcal K_{6B}$ denote the collection of reduced kernels established
in the main text.  A downstream prediction has the form
\[
\mathcal O_{\rm pred}=\mathcal V[\mathcal K_{6B};\mathcal D],
\]
where $\mathcal D$ contains the external propagation or data objects.  If no
entry of $\mathcal D$ is used in the construction of $\mathcal K_{6B}$,
then $\mathcal V$ can validate or falsify the branch but cannot generate a new
Paper--VI-B coefficient.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The reduced kernel is fixed before the external object is supplied.  Therefore
changing the solver, catalog, covariance, or data vector changes only the value
of the pushforward $\mathcal V$, not the upstream definition of
$\mathcal K_{6B}$.  Any feedback from $\mathcal D$ into a coefficient would
instead define a fitted or selected extension of the branch.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Source-to-potential compact propagation benchmark]
\label{p9:prop:v2204-compact-potential-benchmark}

zero-mean potential is
\[
 \Phi_{\rm P}(x)
 =-4\pi G_N\int_{\Sigma_a}G_a^{\rm P}(x,x')\delta\rho(x')\,dV_a(x').

geometric corrections are fixed once the actual source and comparison

constrained kernel and is not replaced by this convolution.

\begin{proof}

$\int\delta\rho\,dV_a=0$. The zero mean fixes the additive potential constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional propagation through the terminal RAR readout]
\label{p9:cor:v2204-rar-propagation-elasticity}

hold $a_{56}=c^2\sqrt{C_{56}}/(4\pi)$ fixed and write
\[
 g_{\rm obs}=g_b\nu(g_b/a_{56}),\qquad

comparison series. Terms quadratic in the small field correction begin at

This propagates a field correction; it does not derive the full globally

\label{p9:prop:v2204-curvature-distance-consistency}

\subsubsection{A cold fermionic fraction has a finite pressure and capacity test}
\begin{proposition}[Finite CAR excitation pressure and cold-capacity bound]
\label{p6b:prop:v2210-lit-cold-car-capacity}
On the free constant-mass source restriction of Paper~VI-A,
Eq.~\eqref{p8:eq:v2171-free-dirac-scalaron-coupling}, use the physical
Jordan frame with fixed masses $m_i\ge0$, an actual complete finite CAR

instantaneous energy blocks, as on an admitted energy-diagonal kinetic
state. Homogeneity alone does not imply this condition. Label each

degeneracy separately by $r$, with admitted unit-S3 eigenvalue $\lambda_r$.

\[
 p_r=|\lambda_r|/a_J,\qquad

\]

\label{p6b:eq:v2210-lit-car-pressure}

\[

\]

\begin{equation}
\label{p6b:eq:v2210-lit-car-cold-capacity}
 \boxed{\frac{\sum_{r:w_r\ge\eta}n_rE_r}{E_{\rm exc}}
                 \le\frac\varepsilon\eta,\qquad
 E_{\rm exc}\le\frac{C_\eta(a_J)}{1-\varepsilon/\eta}.}
\end{equation}

$-\partial_{\log a_J}E_r=p_r^2/E_r$, with all energy-basis/projector
derivatives retained before taking that expectation. The declared
reference/central derivative remains in the separately counted baseline.
This proves the pressure formula and
$p_{\rm exc}/\rho_{\rm exc}=\sum_rn_rE_rw_r/E_{\rm exc}$.
The high-$w_r$ terms contribute at least $\eta$ times their energy

\subsubsection{Actual nuclear transition rates define a conserved positive network}
\begin{proposition}[A conditional finite nuclear continuation certificate]
\label{p6b:prop:v2210-lit-nuclear-network}

\subsubsection{Finite-pulse evaluation on the closed coexact spectrum}

window nor a replacement scalaron history.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The conformal factors cancel from the four-dimensional massless gauge
action. A real orthonormal coexact mode has action
$\frac12\int Z(A_n'^2-n^2A_n^2)\,d\eta$.
For $u_n=\sqrt Z A_n$, integration by parts gives
$u_n''+[n^2-(\sqrt Z)''/\sqrt Z]u_n=0$; the endpoint term vanishes
because the pulse is compactly supported. Apply the mathematical
estimate. Each real out oscillator contributes energy $(n/a_f)N_n$;
multiply by $2d_G(n^2-1)$ and divide by the volume $2\pi^2a_f^3$.
The error follows by the same positive weighted sum. A physical kinetic
member known only to first order retains the second-jet condition of
Remark~\ref{p1b:rem:v215-pulse-jet-domain}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The local width retains its many-shell order of limits]
\label{p9:prop:v215-pulse-rate-order}
In a locally static weak-pump comparison let
$h(t)=\Phi w_T(t)\cos(mt)$ with a smooth long plateau.
Taking the continuum or many-shell limit before the long-time
Golden-Rule limit gives the order-$\lambda^2$ coefficient
\[
 \dot\rho_G=\frac{d_G\lambda^2m^5\Phi^2}{128\pi},\qquad
 \frac{\dot\rho_G}{m^2\Phi^2/2}
                =\frac{d_G\lambda^2m^3}{64\pi}.
\]
A joint flat comparison can use $m^{-1}\ll T\ll a$ and
$|\lambda\Phi|mT\ll1$, retaining negligible depletion and background
drift. At fixed radius and a fixed finite set of modes, an exactly
resonant first-order amplitude instead grows linearly in $T$ and its
squared modulus quadratically; an off-resonant plateau amplitude is
bounded. There is no nonzero finite fixed-window irreversible width
from that discrete monochromatic limit.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Set $k=n/a$. The leading amplitude is
$i\lambda k\int h(t)e^{-2ikt}\,dt$ and its long-plateau squared
modulus per time tends distributionally to
$\pi\lambda^2\Phi^2k^2\delta(2k-m)/2$.
The two-polarization continuum energy measure is
$d_Gk^3\,dk/\pi^2$, hence
\[
 \dot\rho_G=\frac{d_G\lambda^2\Phi^2}{2\pi}
       \int_0^\infty k^5\delta(2k-m)\,dk
       =\frac{d_G\lambda^2m^5\Phi^2}{128\pi}.
\]
The integral of an individual plateau exponential is proportional to
$T$ at exact resonance and bounded by its inverse detuning otherwise.
This proves the finite-window statement and shows why the continuum
replacement is an additional limit. The perturbative coefficient is
not an assertion about holding a fixed pump for arbitrarily long time.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Localization information in cosmological source readouts}
\label{p9:sec:v226-cosmological-information}
\begin{corollary}[A localization-only chiral source inference has a finite bound]
\label{p9:cor:v226-cosmological-information}
If an actually claimed source inference distinguishes the two protected
chiral states using only a measurement with exact intrinsic coordinate
moments and radius $r$, its total-variation distinguishability is at most
$\sqrt{1-3R_{\rm mom}^2/r^2}$. At the optimal radius it is zero.
For a target distinguishability $0\le d<1$, this route necessarily has
$r\ge\sqrt3R_{\rm mom}/\sqrt{1-d^2}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{p2c:thm:v226-chirality-radius} to the actual protected
source comparison and solve its inequality for $r$. Coarse graining cannot
increase total variation. This radius is the classical outcome radius of
that kinematic measurement, not an unproved physical distance calibration.
\end{proof}
The condition does not replace the already generated cyclic root state,
charged source, reheating transfer, baryon-export functional or physical
polarization/detector map. Complete native readouts can use the 137-effect
frame or, with exact position moments at $r>\sqrt3R_{\rm mom}$, the marked
finite construction of Theorem~\ref{p2c:thm:v226-ic-radius-infimum}.
Their availability does not predict a yield, density or history that has
not been evaluated from its actual source and action.

\subsection{Necessary and sufficient finite chiral readout radius}
\label{p6b:sec:v266-chiral-feasibility}
\begin{corollary}[Sharp mathematical radius feasibility at seven outcomes]
\label{p6b:cor:v266-chiral-feasibility}
For the normalized \emph{protected source comparison}
$\rho_\pm=(I_4\pm\chi)/4$, a finite exact-intrinsic-position POVM
with total-variation distinguishability at least $d$, $0\le d<1$,
exists at classical outcome radius $r$ \emph{if and only if}
\[
\boxed{r\ge r_{\min}(d):=
\frac{\sqrt3\Rmom}{\sqrt{1-d^2}}.}
\]
At the lower radius exactly $d$ is attained with at most seven effects.
Six effects attain the bound at $r=r_{\min}(d)$ whenever
$ d\ge\sqrt{7/34}$, while the unrestricted six-effect problem for
$0<d<\sqrt{7/34}$ at its sharp radius is still open.
For $r\ge2\Rmom$ an independent six-effect setting directly reads the
protected chirality observable without geometric-sector
contamination on arbitrary native input states.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Necessity is the existing information bound in
Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:v226-cosmological-information} and
Theorem~\ref{p2c:thm:v226-chirality-radius}.
The seven-effect sharp construction of
Theorem~\ref{p2c:thm:v266-sharp-chirality-support} works at every
$r\ge\sqrt3\Rmom$; its protected total variation equals
$\sqrt{1-3\Rmom^2/r^2}$. The same theorem supplies six effects
for $r\ge r_6=\sqrt{34}\Rmom/3$. At the necessary radius this
condition is equivalent to $d^2\ge7/34$.
The global direct chirality contrast is
Theorem~\ref{p2c:thm:v266-minimal-protected-readout}.
\end{proof}
This equivalence closes a \emph{finite mathematical feasibility}
question only. It does not choose a cosmological source population,
propagate an evolving chiral asymmetry, fix a physical detector,
identify the radius with a cosmological distance, or predict a
measured abundance. At $d=0$ the bound is only a zero-information
feasibility statement, not a nontrivial chirality discriminator.

\subsection{Conditional low-angular-band sampling and leakage certificate}
\label{p6b:sec:cc13-lowell-sampling}

\begin{corollary}[Exact low-multipole coefficient readout on a band-limited sky]
\label{p6b:cor:cc13-sky-sampling}
Let $f$ be an already produced \emph{scalar function} on an observer's
ordinary $S^2$ sky, and suppose the actual visibility/transfer and
observer coordinates yield a band limit $f\in\mathscr Y_6$.
For the specified icosahedral $62$-node rule of
Theorem~\ref{p1b:thm:cc13-band-frame}, all coefficients with
$0\le\ell\le5$ are exactly recovered as
\[
 \boxed{a_{\ell m}=4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{62}
 w_i f(u_i)\overline{Y_{\ell m}(u_i)}.}
\]
In particular, a genuine degree-six component cannot alias into
these recovered degrees.  If the node values have additive errors
$\varepsilon_i$, the vector of recovered coefficient errors on
$\bigoplus_{\ell\le5}\mathscr H_\ell$ obeys the exact operator
norm bound
\[
 \boxed{\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^5\sum_m
 |\delta a_{\ell m}|^2\right)^{1/2}
 \le\left(4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{62}w_i|\varepsilon_i|^2\right)^{1/2}.}
\]
No observed $C_\ell$, cosmic alignment, or anisotropic source is
predicted by these sampling identities.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The coefficient identity is the degree-eleven cubature theorem,
because $\overline{Y_{\ell m}}f$ has degree at most $\ell+6\le11$.
For the error estimate, use the orthonormal spherical-harmonic
basis and the $36\times62$ sampling matrix
$B_{\ell m,i}=\sqrt{4\pi w_i}Y_{\ell m}(u_i)$,
which satisfies $BB^*=I_{36}$ by the source isometry.
The noise-to-coefficient map is the conjugate matrix $\bar B$,
of operator norm one, applied to the vector
$\big(\sqrt{4\pi w_i}\varepsilon_i\big)_i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the band-limit hypothesis]
Paper VI-B's native momentum-harmonic and sky transfer objects cannot
be identified just because their index symbols look similar.  This
sampling result assumes a specified \emph{observed sky function}\;
it does not establish a source-owned transfer or a physical six-band cutoff.  General higher modes $\ell\ge7$ can alias, and
the degree-six-by-degree-six Gram is not exact because the cubature
fails on a degree-twelve invariant harmonic.
\end{remark}
