\section{Differential rigidity of the conserved closed comparison family}
\label{p9:sec:cc14-kinematic-rigidity}
\begin{theorem}[Fourth-order Euler null and local coefficient inversion]
\label{p9:thm:cc14-kinematic-euler}
On a connected interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, a $C^4$ function
$F(y)$ has the conserved dust--curvature--vacuum--radiation
comparison form
\[
 F(y)=A_0+A_2y^2+A_3y^3+A_4y^4
\]
with constant coefficients if and only if, for $D=y\partial_y$,
\[
 \boxed{D(D-2)(D-3)(D-4)F=0.}
\]
The four coefficients are reconstructed \emph{locally} by
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 A_0&=-\tfrac1{24}(D-2)(D-3)(D-4)F,\\
 A_2y^2&=\tfrac14D(D-3)(D-4)F,\\
 A_3y^3&=-\tfrac13D(D-2)(D-4)F,\\
 A_4y^4&=\tfrac18D(D-2)(D-3)F.
 \end{aligned}}
\]
No unknown density coefficient remains in the null operator.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Every monomial $y^m$ is an eigenfunction of $D$ with eigenvalue
$m$, so the operator annihilates precisely the four displayed
monomial types.  Conversely, set $u=\log y$; then
$D=\partial_u$, and the homogeneous constant-coefficient ODE has
four distinct characteristic roots $0,2,3,4$, giving exactly the
claimed solution space.  The coefficient identities are the
Lagrange spectral projectors
$\prod_{n\ne m}(D-n)/(m-n)$ applied to $F$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deceleration--jerk early-history discriminant]
\label{p9:cor:cc14-qj-history}
On the strictly closed, conserved dust--vacuum subbranch at the
observer reference $y=1$, assume $A_0>0$, $A_2<0$, $A_3>0$ and
$A_4=0$.  With $q_0,j_0$ as in
Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:v358-kinematic-null}, the necessary
sign domain is
\[
 \boxed{q_0+j_0>0,\qquad j_0-2q_0>0,\qquad j_0>1.}
\]
The connected all-$y>0$ expanding comparison history criterion of
Corollary~\ref{p9:cor:v358-observer-invariant-history} is exactly
\[
 \boxed{(j_0-2q_0)(q_0+j_0)^2-(j_0-1)^3>0.}
\]
Equality gives the critical zero of $H^2$; the opposite inequality
gives a forbidden interval of positive redshift coordinate in the
full comparison polynomial.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The cited source gives
$\Omega_m=2(q_0+j_0)/3$,
$\Omega_\Lambda=(j_0-2q_0)/3$,
$\Omega_k=1-j_0$; hence the signs.  In the source comparison,
$\mathcal E_{\rm conn}=4(-A_2)^3/(27A_0A_3^2)$, so substitution
and cancellation of the positive $H_0^6$ gives
$\mathcal E_{\rm conn}=(j_0-1)^3/
[(j_0-2q_0)(q_0+j_0)^2]$.  The strict criterion
$\mathcal E_{\rm conn}<1$ is precisely the claimed inequality.
\end{proof}
\begin{remark}[Scope and derivative conditioning]
The Euler null tests the stipulated \emph{separately conserved
comparison family}, not actual cosmic expansion without the
physical-history incidence certificate.  A fourth derivative is
numerically unstable with uncalibrated noisy data; Paper VI-B's
existing finite-window reconstruction is the appropriate stabilized
comparison.  The $q_0,j_0$ discriminant applies only when the
radiation term is absent or separately removed; it is not a
universal observational history selector.
\end{remark}

% BEGIN CROSS-DAG v1.5: derivative-free finite-redshift null
\begin{theorem}[Five-point derivative-free conserved-component null]
\label{p9:thm:cc15-five-point-null}
Let $I\subset(0,\infty)$ be an interval and
$v(y)=(1,y^2,y^3,y^4)$. For any four distinct points
$y_1,\ldots,y_4\in I$, the generalized Vandermonde matrix
$V=[v(y_i)]_{i=1}^4$ is invertible. A function $F:I\to\mathbb R$
therefore belongs to the four-component conserved family
$F(y)=A_0+A_2y^2+A_3y^3+A_4y^4$ \emph{if and only if} the
five-point determinant
\[
 \boxed{\det\!\begin{pmatrix}
 1&y_1^2&y_1^3&y_1^4&F(y_1)\\
 1&y_2^2&y_2^3&y_2^4&F(y_2)\\
 1&y_3^2&y_3^3&y_3^4&F(y_3)\\
 1&y_4^2&y_4^3&y_4^4&F(y_4)\\
 1&y_5^2&y_5^3&y_5^4&F(y_5)
 \end{pmatrix}=0}
\]
holds for \emph{every} fifth point $y_5\in I$ distinct from the
four fixed ones. In particular a single nonzero five-point
determinant falsifies this exact comparison family on those data.
If $F$ belongs to this exact comparison family, then for
independently calibrated observations $F_i^{\rm obs}$ with
$|F_i^{\rm obs}-F(y_i)|\le\varepsilon_i$, the null-prediction
residual satisfies the sharp elementary stability estimate
\[
 \boxed{\left|F_5^{\rm obs}
 -v(y_5)V^{-1}(F_1^{\rm obs},\ldots,F_4^{\rm obs})^{\mathsf T}\right|
 \le\varepsilon_5+\sum_{i=1}^{4}
  |(v(y_5)V^{-1})_i|\,\varepsilon_i.}
\]
This is a finite-data comparison/falsification bound, not a proof of
physical occurrence from finitely many agreeing points.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The monomials $y^0,y^2,y^3,y^4$ form an extended complete
Chebyshev system on $(0,\infty)$: their full Wronskian is
$\prod_{i<j}(e_j-e_i)y^{\sum e_i-6}=48y^3>0$, and the lower
initial Wronskians are positive as well. Thus $V$ is invertible
for any four distinct positive nodes.
If $F=vA$, the fifth column is a linear combination of the first
four, so every determinant vanishes. Conversely fix four nodes,
solve $A=V^{-1}(F(y_1),\ldots,F(y_4))$, and use the displayed
determinant for every fifth node to infer $F(y_5)=v(y_5)A$.
The error estimate is the triangle inequality after subtracting
this exact interpolation identity and uses no derivative estimates.
\end{proof}
\begin{remark}[One null sample is not global confirmation]
The observed family must satisfy the same source's strict coefficient
sign and physical-history hypotheses before any connected-history
interpretation. Agreement at one five-tuple is necessary but not
sufficient for the entire redshift curve. The noise estimate assumes
known calibrated point values; a real instrument with windowed data
uses the moment matrix of
Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:v358-window-reconstruction} instead.
\end{remark}
% END CROSS-DAG v1.5
% END SOURCE-INTEGRATED CROSS-PAPER MATHEMATICS v3_017.

% BEGIN FRAMEWORK v2.356: exact inverse closed comparison map
\section{Inverse map for the closed dust--vacuum comparison family}
\label{p9:sec:v356-closed-comparison-inverse}

\begin{theorem}[Exact comparison-family inversion]
\label{p9:thm:v356-closed-comparison-inverse}
On the late conserved-dust plus constant-vacuum domain of
Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:closed-curvature-family-repaired}, suppose a
comparison density triplet obeys
$\Omega_m>0$, $\Omega_\Lambda>0$, $\Omega_k<0$ and
$\Omega_m+\Omega_\Lambda+\Omega_k=1$.  Set
\[
 r=\frac{\Omega_m}{\Omega_\Lambda},\qquad
 x=\frac{-\Omega_k}{1-\Omega_k}.
\]
If $0<r\le2$ and $0<x<1$, there is exactly one corresponding
comparison pair $(N,d)$ with $d\ge0$:
\[
 \boxed{d=\tfrac13\log\frac2r,\qquad
 N=\tfrac72\log\alpha-d-\tfrac12\log[x(1+r)].}
\]
The connected early expanding-history criterion becomes the
exact test
\[
 \boxed{x<\frac{3(r/2)^{2/3}}{1+r}.}
\]
The positive Hubble normalization on this comparison reduction is
$H_0^2=\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2/(3\Omega_\Lambda)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The forward map gives $r=2e^{-3d}$ and
$\Omega_k=-x/(1-x)$; both inversions are strictly one-to-one on
the stated domains.  Solving the definition of $x(N;d)$ for $N$
gives the displayed logarithm.  Substitute the inversion of $d$
into $N>N_{\rm crit}=\tfrac72\log\alpha-\tfrac12\log3$
to obtain the history inequality.  Finally the density definition
$\Omega_\Lambda=\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2/(3H_0^2)$ gives the Hubble
identity.  The density-sum constraint ensures the recovered triplet
is the given one.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[No new actual observation-slice selection]
\label{p9:rem:v356-inversion-not-occurrence}
The inverse map is an algebraic identity inside a \emph{comparison}
family.  It does not derive the actual present epoch, choose $d$, or
certify an exchange history, whole-action Newton normalization or
metrological realization from G0 and GP1--GP3.
\end{remark}
% END FRAMEWORK v2.356


% BEGIN FRAMEWORK v2.358: kinematic, calibrated-window and optical tests (DAG v1.19-v1.21)
\section{Conditional kinematic and finite-window cosmological null tests}
\label{p9:sec:v358-observable-kinematic-tests}
\begin{proposition}[Conserved-component kinematic vacuum null]
\label{p9:prop:v358-kinematic-null}
On the separately conserved closed dust--curvature--vacuum
comparison branch of Theorem~\ref{p9:thm:closed-curvature-family-repaired},
allow also an independently conserved radiation term and write
$y=1+z>0$ and
\[
 F(y):=H(y)^2=A_0+A_2y^2+A_3y^3+A_4y^4,
 \quad A_0>0,\quad A_2<0,\quad A_3>0,\quad A_4\ge0.
\]
Define the kinematic deceleration and jerk on the corresponding
expanding smooth branch by
\[
 q=-1+\frac{yF'}{2F},\qquad
 j=1-\frac{yF'}F+\frac{y^2F''}{2F},\qquad
 \Omega_r(y)=\frac{A_4y^4}{F(y)}.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v358-kinematic-vacuum-null}
 H(y)^2\bigl[j(y)-2q(y)-\Omega_r(y)\bigr]
 =3A_0=\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2
\end{equation}
when $A_0=\Lambda_{\rm eff}c^2/3$.  On the dust--vacuum
subbranch $A_4=0$, the observer-reference values satisfy
$\Omega_{m,0}=\frac23(q_0+j_0)$,
$\Omega_{\Lambda,0}=\frac13(j_0-2q_0)$ and
$\Omega_{k,0}=1-j_0$.
These identities do not assert that the occurring geometric
expansion obeys the comparison polynomial.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Substitution gives
$F(j-2q)=3F-2yF'+y^2F''/2=3A_0+A_4y^4$.
Subtract $F\Omega_r=A_4y^4$ and use the standard conserved
comparison definitions at the reference event.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-window identifiability and stability]
\label{p9:thm:v358-window-reconstruction}
Suppose, as an \emph{additional calibrated instrument protocol},
that the $i$th datum is $b_i=\int F(y)\,dW_i(y)$ for known positive
normalized observation windows $W_i$, and initially set $A_4=0$.
Write $A=(A_0,A_2,A_3)^\mathsf T$ and
$M_{ik}=\int y^k\,dW_i(y)$, $k\in\{0,2,3\}$.  Then
$b=MA$ and the coefficients are locally uniquely determined in the
open admissible parameter domain precisely when $\operatorname{rank}M=3$.
When $M$ has full column rank, a least-squares reconstruction obeys
$\|\delta A\|\le\|\delta b\|/\sigma_{\min}(M)$.

For three normalized equal-half-width top-hat windows of half-width
$h>0$ and distinct centers $h<y_1<y_2<y_3$, one has
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v358-window-determinant}
\det M=\prod_{i<j}(y_j-y_i)
\left(y_1y_2+y_1y_3+y_2y_3-h^2\right)>0.
\end{equation}
If the radiation coefficient $A_4$ is unknown, add the fourth
moment column $\int y^4dW_i$ and require rank four, or subtract a
separately known radiation term first.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The moment decomposition is linear.  Full column rank is equivalent
to injectivity; rank deficiency permits arbitrarily small
coefficient shifts in the null direction while staying in the
open admissible domain.  The pseudoinverse norm is
$1/\sigma_{\min}(M)$.  A symmetric top hat satisfies
$\int y^2dW_i=y_i^2+h^2/3$ and
$\int y^3dW_i=y_i^3+y_ih^2$.
Subtracting the first row from the other two and factoring the
differences yields Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:v358-window-determinant}.
For $y_i>h$, each pair product exceeds $h^2$, giving positivity.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Reference-independent connected comparison test]
\label{p9:cor:v358-observer-invariant-history}
On the strict closed dust--vacuum domain $A_0>0,A_2<0,A_3>0$,
the polynomial has positive $F(y)$ for all $y>0$ precisely when
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v358-connected-history}
 \mathcal E_{\rm conn}:=\frac{4(-A_2)^3}{27A_0A_3^2}<1.
\end{equation}
The dimensionless number is invariant under a change of redshift
reference $y'=\lambda y$, $\lambda>0$, with the coefficient and
window relabelling $A_k'=\lambda^{-k}A_k$.  The equality case has a
zero of $H^2$, so strict positivity requires the strict inequality.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The positive stationary point of $F=A_0+A_2y^2+A_3y^3$ is
$y_*=-2A_2/(3A_3)$, where
$F(y_*)=A_0+4A_2^3/(27A_3^2)$.  Since the boundary
and large-$y$ values are positive, strict positivity is equivalent
to Eq.~\eqref{p9:eq:v358-connected-history}.
The coefficient scaling cancels in the ratio.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Metric-optical curvature without distance scale]
\label{p9:prop:v358-optical-two-redshift}
On the \emph{conditional} closed-FLRW metric photon/reciprocity branch
of Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:v2204-curvature-distance-consistency},
write $D=D_M=D_L/(1+z)$ and assume the same calibrated $H(z)$ and
$c$ over the redshift interval.  For $D\ne0$, metric-null transport
gives
\[
 \mathcal K_{\rm obs}(z)=
 \frac{1-(HD'/c)^2}{D^2}=K>0,
 \qquad K=a_0^{-2}.
\]
If the measured distance is $D_{\rm obs}=\lambda D$ for one
unknown \emph{constant} $\lambda>0$, define
$X=D_{\rm obs}^2$, $Y=(HD_{\rm obs}'/c)^2$.
Then $Y=\lambda^2-KX$ and for $X_1\ne X_2$,
\begin{equation}
\label{p9:eq:v358-optical-calibration}
 K=\frac{Y_1-Y_2}{X_2-X_1},\qquad
 \lambda^2=\frac{Y_1X_2-Y_2X_1}{X_2-X_1}.
\end{equation}
This eliminates only a single constant distance normalization;
it does not generate the physical photon Hessian, the emitter,
observer, redshift/clock transfer or photon-number conservation.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The round closed-FLRW radial distance law is
$D=a_0\sin\chi$ with $\chi' =c/(a_0H)$.  Differentiate to
obtain $(HD'/c)^2=\cos^2\chi=1-KD^2$ and substitute
$D_{\rm obs}=\lambda D$.  Solve the resulting two linear equations.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Retained no-present theorem]
\label{p9:rem:v358-no-present-preserved}
No formula in this section produces a privileged physical present
slice.  Proposition~\ref{p9:prop:present-slice-no-go} and its
observer/state/detector obligations remain intact.  A general
survey pushforward is not automatically the linear window average
assumed above; that is a separate measurable instrument hypothesis.
\end{remark}

\section{Berger shape as a multi-operator null test}
\label{bh:berger:v117:p6b:readout}
\begin{proposition}[Mass-independent static geometry consistency test]
\label{bh:berger:v117:p6b:heat-gauge}
For compatible, identified static/comoving spatial gauge modes on
one and the same volume-preserving Berger metric, define
\[
 r=\frac{\omega_{2,0}^2-\omega_{2,\pm2}^2}
         {\omega_{2,0}^2-\omega_{1,\pm1}^2},
 \qquad h=\frac{a_2}{2\pi^2a},
\]
where $a_2$ is the \emph{scalar Laplacian} heat coefficient,
not the full interacting Dirac/gauge heat trace. Then
\[
 r=\frac{4(e^{2s}-1)}{6e^{2s}-1},\quad
 h(r)=\frac{12-23r}{3(4-6r)}
              \left(\frac{4-r}{4-6r}\right)^{1/3},\quad
 h=1-\frac{25}{72}r^2+O(r^3).
\]
The ratio is defined on the open domain where its denominator is nonzero.
The gauge mass cancels in the frequency-difference ratio. This is a
geometric consistency relation, not a prediction of observed $W/Z$
masses, a CMB signal, or a physical occupied deformation.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Insert the already established Hopf-weight-resolved scalar
spectrum into the longitudinal gauge operator with its common
mass shift; subtract squared frequencies so the mass cancels.
Eliminate $e^{2s}$ using the displayed rational $r$ and the exact
Berger heat coefficient
$h=(4e^{2s/3}-e^{8s/3})/3$.
\end{proof}
\begin{remark}[Sixth-order angular and detector ownership]
\label{bh:berger:v117:p6b:t6}
The marked ten-axis sextic $T_6$ retains its established angular
shape on the appropriate icosahedral packet; its amplitude and
observer transfer require an actual anisotropic source, coupled
history and detector. In a first-order source-transport chart, the
physical metric and gauge/detector operators must be reconstructed
before applying this or any other spectral relation.
\end{remark}
